Annual Report 2005                                                                                                    Economic Situation of the Republic of Moldova

1.Economic Situation of the Republic of Moldova   
World Economy in 2005 
The world economy continued to expand over 2005 mainly due to the 
economic activity displayed by the United States of America and 
Asia. Although prices for energy products exhibited ascendant trends 
over the reported year, inflationary pressures have, in general terms, 
been constrained. The world economic growth in 2005 has been 
estimated at 4.3%. 
Economic growth of the USA accounted for 
3.5% in 2005 (as in accordance with 
estimated values), reducing by 0.7 percentage 
point from 2004. Annual average inflation 
rose to 3.4%, reaching the highest level over 
the last five years. Unemployment rate 
exhibited descendant trends for the second 
consecutive year, accounting for 5.1%.  
Within Euro zone GDP growth over 2005 
slowed down by 0.8 percentage point as 
compared to 2004 and constituted 1.3% as 
according to estimates. Consumer prices 
increased by 2.2% from the previous year and 
unemployment rate slightly reduced to 8.6%. 
The real GDP growth within the 25 EU 
countries augmented by 1.6% from 2004; 
inflation rate kept the same level of 2.2% registered in the Euro zone. 
Unemployment rate decreased by 0.4 percentage point down to 8.7%.  
Economic performance in Japan intensified in the first half of 2005, 
and, as estimated, exceeded by 2.7% the results of 2004. At the same 
time, monetary authorities did not manage to combat the deflation 
phenomenon neither in 2005: consumer prices reduced by 0.3% from 
the previous year.  
Chinese economy denoted high growth rates like in previous years: 
GDP rose by 9.9%, and inflation accounted for 1.8%. 
Romania, according to relevant forecast, displayed an economic 
growth of 4.1% on the background of a 9.0% average annual inflation 
rate.  
In Russia, GDP augmented by 6.4%. Inflation accounted for 10.9% as 
at year-end, which is by 0.8 percentage point less than in 2004. 
Ukrainian economy exhibited a reduction of economic growth down 
to 2.4% from 12.1% in 2004. Inflation accounted for 10.3%, 
diminishing by 2.0 percentage points from 2004.

4
International foreign exchange market. On the background of 
frequent rises of the US$ base rate and ultimate enlargement of 
discrepancies between base rates of other currencies in favour of US$, 
in 2005 US$ displayed a general appreciation trend as against other 
currencies. The largest US$ appreciation against Euro has been noted 
from May to June 2005.

Real Sector∗ 
Economic development of the Republic of Moldova in 2005 exhibited 
maintained macroeconomic stability: both industrial and agricultural 
output registered increased values – by 6.3% and 1.0%, respectively; 
investments intensified; and economic growth 
ultimately accounted for 7.1% (chart no. 1). 
Gross Domestic Product (GDP) amounted to 
36755.4 million lei in current prices in 2005. 
GDP 
per 
capita 
totalled 
10225.3 
lei 
(equivalent 
of 
around 
US$ 
811.5), 
augmenting by 15.0% in nominal terms from 
the previous year. GDP deflator, which 
characterises the dynamics of general price 
level within economy, accounted for 7.2% (as 
compared to 8.0% in 2004).  
According to GDP by resources, in 2005 
GDP components displayed more reserved 
trends as compared to 2004: gross added 
value enlarged by 4.5% as against 8.3% in 
2004, including within production of goods – 
by 2.9% (in industry – by 4.1% and in the 
agricultural sector – by 1.8%); and services – 
by 5.1%. Net taxes on goods and 
imports 
recorded 
a 
significant 
increase 
of 
22.5% 
from 
the 
previous year (2.1% in 2004). 
Within GDP structure, components 
followed the same tendencies as 
over 
previous 
years: 
services 
accounted for the largest share of 
54.4%; agriculture accounted for 
14.2% of GDP (17.6% in 2004); 
industry – for 17.0%; net taxes on 
goods and imports – for 16.4% 
(14.1% in 2004); and financial 
intermediation services indirectly 
measured (FISIM) – minus 2.0%. 
Within GDP real growth, the contribution of supply components was 
as follows: net taxes accounted for 3,2 percentage points; services – 
for 2.7 percentage points; and industry and agriculture – for 0.7 and 
0.3 percentage point, respectively (chart no. 2).

Within GDP breakdown by use, the risen 
by 9.8% domestic aggregate demand 
implied the increase by 9.5% of final 
consumption (including by households – 
by 10.1%) and by 11.0% of gross capital 
formation (including of fixed capital 
gross formation – by 10.2%). Net exports 
of goods and services displayed a 0.5 
percentage point expansion of imports’ 
real 
growth 
over 
exports, 
thus 
contributing with minus 5.7 percentage 
points to GDP real growth (chart no. 3).          
Within GDP structure by use category, 
final consumption rose by 4.5 percentage 
points from 2004 and accounted for 
108.4%. At the same time, gross capital 
formation consolidated to 29.8% of GDP 
value, mainly supported by the growth to 
23.1% of the share of gross savings 
(chart no.4).  
The investment rate, determined as ratio 
of gross fixed capital formation to gross 
added value in the economy, accounted 
for 29.1% (as compared to 24.7% in 
2004 and 21.8% in 2003) 
The ratio between domestic supply and 
domestic aggregate demand constituted 
60.5%, by 5.4 percentage points less 
than in 2004.  
In 2005 industrial output produced by 
enterprises 
of 
all 
property 
forms 
exhibited a 6.3% real increase from the 
previous year, amounting to 21123.5 
million lei in current prices. Despite the 
slowing down of the growth rate over 
the last two years (from 15.6% in 2003 
to 6.3% in 2005), the value of industrial 
output over 2000 to 2005 rose by 80.4% 
in real terms as compared to 1999.  
The volume of industrial output by large and medium enterprises 
subject to monthly statistics recording totalled 16194.6 million lei, 
augmenting by 6.3% in real terms (chart no. 5) mainly due to the 
processing industry, which accounted for 4.9 percentage points of

and private sectors (34.5% and 35.0%, respectively), which displayed 
increases of 14.0% and 21.0%.  
The value of investments in the production sector accounted for 
68.4% of total investments in fixed capital, keeping the level of the 
previous year. Investments in the non-production sector increased by 
32.0% and totalled 2069.3 million lei.  
By source of investments, own funds (including population means) 
accounted for the largest share of 68.9% of total investments, of which 
35.0% has been allocated into public property and 45.9% - into private 
property (chart no. 9). Investments on account of the republican and 
local budget accounted for 8.6% of fixed capital investments, of which 
80.2% went into the non-production sector.    
Fixed funds put into circulation in 2005 amounted to 4279.4 million 
lei, of which 73.1% - in the production sector.  
Short-term accounts receivable totalled 22717.2 million lei as of 30 
September 2005, including 20139.4 million lei (88.7%) on 
commercial invoices. The stock of short-term claims rose by 21.5% 
from the end of 2004.  
Short-term accounts payable of enterprises grew by 7.6% and 
amounted, as of 30 September 2005, to 48368.9 million lei. Short-
term accounts receivable and accounts payable of economic agents in 
breakdown by economic activity are shown in chart no. 10.

The total value of retail trade expanded by 4.7% in real terms from 
the previous year and totalled 19368.2 million, mainly due to the 
11.6% increase of trade with non-food products (63.8% of total sales). 
At the same time, trading with food products reduced by 5.2%.  
The 2005 value of retail trade through commercial units amounted to 
10939.8 million lei, surpassing by 13.0% the value in the previous 
year. Reduced value (by 3.8%) of retail trade has been registered only 
within trade units of public property (chart no. 11). The largest trade 
turnover has been conducted through private commercial units (68.8% 
of total trade), within which the relevant growth accounted for 12.4%.    
The value of goods traded over 2005 in domestic markets amounted to 
8428.4 million lei (according to estimates), reducing by 4.4% from the 
previous year.   
The value of services to the population rendered through the formal 
sector (81.0% of total services) rose by 8.5% in real terms and totalled 
6635.0 million. Augmented values of rendered services have been 
registered in all forms of property; public units accounted for 46.2% 
of total services (chart no. 11).  
Services 
rendered 
by 
individuals 
amounted to 1559.2 million lei, by 
12.8% more than in 2004. 
Economically active population (as in 
accordance with data of quarterly 
surveys covering 8208 households) 
totalled 1422.3 thousand persons, 
reducing by 0.7% from 2004. The 
activity rate∗ of persons aging 15 years 
old and older accounted for 49.0%. 
Unemployment, as in accordance with 
data of International Labour Office 
(ILO) totalled around 103.7 thousand 
persons (reducing by 12.8 thousand persons as compared to 2004). 
Unemployment rate accounted for 7.3% (8.1% in 2004).    
The number of employees in national economy∗∗ (662.8 thousand 
persons) diminished by 4.3 thousand persons (0.7%). The largest staff 
reduction over the last years has been observed in the agricultural 
sector (by 8.4% in 2005), which is mainly due to modified conditions 
in the labour market and exhibited tendencies of staff reduction. In 
consequence, rises have been noted in the number of persons in search 
for work both in the domestic and external markets. At the same time,

amounted to 9336.4 million lei. Wage 
increases were noted over the year in all

accordance with the Decision of the Government of the Republic of 
Moldova no. 678 of 11 July 2005 on Liquidation of Wage Arrears in 
the Real Sector of Economy.

canned meat – by 14.3%; and milk and diary products – by 10.2%.  
Prices for non-food products registered in 2005 the largest increase of 
14.9%, accounting for 4.4 percentage points of inflation rate. Within 
this context, considerable price augmentations were recorded for fuel 
– by 35.7%; medicines – by 17.3%; construction materials – by 
14.6%, ready-made cloths – by 10.8%; and footwear – by 10.8%. 
Fuel prices in the European market augmented in December 2005 by 
61.7% as compared to December 2004. 
Within services rendered to the population, price and tariff increase 
denoted the slowest growth over the last three years – by 6.6%, 
accounting for 16.0% of consumer price general index increase. 
Within this group, the most significant 
price rises have been registered within 
dwelling reparation and maintenance – by 
30.7%; public alimentation – by 27.0%; 
passengers’ transportation – by 14.2%; and 
education – by 7.7%.  
Industrial producer prices over the year 
rose by 7.2%, which is by 2.8 percentage 
points less than the consumer price level, 
and yet, by 2.3 percentage points more than 
the level in 2004 (chart no. 14). Within 
mining sector prices increased by 25.9%; 
in processing industry – by 7.5%; and in 
electricity, heating gas and water – by 
5.2%. 
Noticeable 
price 
hikes 
were 
exhibited in the following activities: 
production of bricks and tiles – by 50.7%; 
sugar production – by 26.0%; production 
of plastic and rubber items – by 23.0%; 
wood processing and production of 
wooden items – by 20.4%; production of 
pumps, 
compressors 
and 
hydraulic 
systems – by 19.5%; and fruits and 
vegetables processing and preserving – by 
17.0%.           
The average monthly living subsistence in 
economy in 2005 amounted to 766.1 lei per person (chart no. 15). As 
compared to the previous year, the value of living subsistence 
enlarged by 12.7% in nominal terms and varied depending of price 
seasonal fluctuations pertinent to relevant year periods. 
In breakdown by category of population, the maximum value of living 
subsistence has been registered by economically active population

12
(809.2 lei), and especially by men (856.7 lei). Child support implies a 
monthly allocation of 715.6 lei, providing due differentiation of this 
indicator depending on child age. The average monthly pension 
totalled 383.4 lei following the 18.0% increase in nominal terms over 
the year of the average monthly pension (depending on rises in 
consumer prices and minimal wages in economy). This pension 
covered the living subsistence related to this category of citizens 
(649.1 lei) by 59.1%. 
The ratio between the average wage of an employee in the national 
economy (1318.7 lei) and the living subsistence accounted for 
172.1%. The ratio between average disposable income per capita and 
living subsistence constituted 74.2%.  
As in accordance with households’ surveys, the average monthly 
disposable income in 2005 per capita amounted to 568.6 lei, denoting 
a 3.4% increase from the previous year.  
Average monthly expenditures per capita totalled 519.5 lei in 2005, 
reducing by 3.1% in real terms from 2004.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


Annual Report 2005                                                                                                    Economic Situation of the Republic of Moldova 
 
13
Public Sector Finance∗ 
  
In 2005 the national public budget was projected as follows: budget 
revenues – 14602.2 million lei; and budget expenditures – 15029.0 
million lei.  
National public budget revenues, as in accordance with preliminary 
data, amounted to 14694.9 million lei, or 100.6% of annual projected 
values. As compared to the previous year, total revenues increase by 
28.8%, or 3287.3 million lei. Following execution operative data, 
consolidated budget expenditures over the year amounted to 13937.2 
million lei, or 92.7% of annual projections. The national public 
budget registered a surplus of 757.7 million lei.  
Tax collections (82.0% of total revenues) valued 12052.9 million lei, 
or 100.5% of set projections. Within the structure of national public 
budget revenues, indirect taxes accounted for 44.1%; direct taxes – 
for 35.9%; and other revenues – for 20.0%.  
Non-fiscal revenues totalled 2006.2 million lei, or 97.1% of set 
projections, accounting for 13.7% of total national public budget 
collected revenues. Revenues from capital transactions and grants 
amounted to 176.3 million lei and 459.5 million lei, respectively. 
Total state budget revenues in 2005 amounted to 9053.0 million lei 
(100.3% of set provisions stipulated in the Law on the State Budget 
no. 373-XV of 11 November 2004 and further amendments of 8 
December 2005 no. 312-XVI), surpassing by 2485.9 million lei 
(37.9%) the value registered in 2004. State budget expenditures 
totalled 8470.2 million lei (93.9% of annual projections), exceeding 
by 27.3% the value of expenditures in 2004.  
The state budget was closed in 2005 with a surplus of 582.8 million 
lei against the value of 10.3 million lei as provided in the budget.  
State budget base revenues amounted to 7794.0 million lei, 
surpassing by 2.5% set annual projections. State budget base 
expenditures totalled 6954.0 million lei, by 0.2% more than annual 
projections. The base state budget was closed with a surplus of 840.0 
million lei.  
Revenues accumulated in 2005 for budgets of territorial-
administrative units amounted to 3785.0 million lei, or 103.3% of 
annual projections. Expenditures valued 3782.0 million lei, or 95.4% 
of set projections. Overall, budgets of territorial-administrative units 
registered a surplus of 3.0 million lei.  
                                                 
∗ Source: preliminary data of the Ministry of Finance of the Republic of Moldova  


Annual Report 2005                                                                                                    Economic Situation of the Republic of Moldova 
 
14
Domestic public debt as of 31 December 2005 totalled 3787.1 million 
lei and complied with the limits provided in the Law on the State 
Budget for 2005. 
Within total domestic debt, credits of the National Bank of Moldova 
amounted to 2211.04 million lei and outstanding state securities – to 
1576.02 million lei (including in NBM portfolio - 400.0 million lei).

of

continuous ascendant trends (chart no. 17), reaching a 41.5%-weight

11.8

and accounted for

to

ratio 
exhibited 
a

points, denoting the 
amplification 
of 
national economy’s dependence on 
external factors.  
The deficit of goods accounts with 
the rest of the world increased by 
67.2% from 2004 and totalled US$ 
850.6 million. The negative balance 
with CIS countries amounted to US$ 
340.2 million, displaying a 38.6%-
rise from the previous year.  
The largest trade with goods deficits 
have been recorded in trade with 
Ukraine, Romania, and Germany 
(table no. 5).

US$ 1104.4 million (chart no. 18), 
of which exports to CIS countries

US$ 543.1 million (12.8%).

(4.3%); 
USA 
(3.4%); Poland (2.3%); and Turkey

and

products 
constituted more than half of exported

increase from the previous year.

(FOB prices) over 
the reported period 
grew by 31.3% and

million (chart no.

(13.2%);

(7.6%); Italy (5.4%): Belarus 
(3.7%); Turkey (3.6%); Poland 
(3.4%); and France (2.1%) 
(table no. 7).

22
principal repayments (US$ 167.4 million) surpassed disbursements 
(US$ 165.6 million).  
Other liabilities in the form of governmental external arrears 
increased by US$ 16.9 million in net value; and external arrears of 
other sectors – by US$ 11.4 million. Thus, as of 2005-end, the stock 
of governmental arrears totalled US$ 36.8 million; and the stock of 
other sectors’ arrears – US$ 397.7 million.  
State reserve assets increased over 2005 by US$ 128.6 million in net 
terms (chart no. 21). Out of reserves an amount of US$ 76.4 million 
was used for external debt service, US$ 10.0 million - for 
maintenance of embassies, US$ 4.0 million - for payments of fees to 
international organizations. The domestic foreign exchange market, 
where NBM bought foreign currencies in an amount of US$ 174.8 
million, still remained the main source of reserves replenishment. 
The reserve assets’ stock as of 2005-end totalled US$ 597.4 million. 
The level of reserve assets as of 31 December 2005 was sufficient to 
cover current imports of goods and services for 2.6 months.

External Debt of the Republic of Moldova  
The gross external debt of Moldova at the end of 2005 amounted to 
US$1991.0 million, 37.7% of which were short-term liabilities 
(table no. 14). 
Other sectors and the governmental sector 
have furtheron accounted for the largest shares 
in external liabilities – 44.4% and 33.0%, 
respectively (chart no. 22).     
The public and publicly guaranteed debt 
amounted to US$773.7 million, reducing by 
12.0% from the previous year (table no. 15). 
External private non-guaranteed debt totalled 
US$1217.3 million, enlarging by 22.4% from 
the end of 2004, mainly due to drawings on 
new external loans from affiliated companies 
and accumulation of trade credits provided by 
non-residents.

The stock of NBM liabilities to IMF diminished by 24.2%. 
Reimbursements on IMF loans amounted to US$ 21.4 million.  
Direct governmental debt reduced by 5.6%. The governmental sector 
benefited from new borrowings in an amount of US$ 25.9 million, of 
which US$ 23.6 million were received from IDA within previously 
initiated projects. The actual payments on principal of loans and 
securities constituted US$ 29.4 million against scheduled principal 
payments in the amount of US$ 45.2 million. Arrears on principal on 
bilateral credits amounted to US$ 15.8 million.  
Private external liabilities under government guarantee reduced by 
45.9%. Real principal payments totalled US$ 10.4 million.  
The private sector benefited over the reported year from new 
external loans in the amount of US$ 249.4 million. US$ 235.5 
million were received by enterprises from other sector  (of which 
US$ 109.8 million - from parent companies); other funds - by the 
commercial banks. The real principal payments of the private sector 
totalled US$ 134.9 million. 
The dynamics of relevant indicators revealing the state of national 
economy indebtedness is shown in table no.18.

Annual Report 2005                                                                                                                Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
27
 2. Activity of the National Bank of Moldova   
 
 
Monetary 
and 
Foreign 
Exchange 
Policy 
Implementation in 2005 
 
Monetary Policy Objectives and Results  
 
The establishment and promotion of the monetary and foreign 
exchange policy under the scope of the economic policy of the State 
represent one of the basic attributions of the National Bank of 
Moldova. The monetary and foreign exchange policy is defined as a 
set of measures undertaken by the National Bank of Moldova for 
purposes of establishing adequate monetary conditions favourable for 
the maintenance of macroeconomic stability and supporting of 
sustained economic growth.  
The main objective of the National Bank implies the achievement and 
maintenance of the stability of the national currency. To reach this 
target, the National Bank establishes and maintains monetary, credit 
and foreign exchange market conditions conducive to the orderly and 
balanced economic development of the State and especially, of the 
market-based financial and foreign exchange system. 
The monetary and foreign exchange policy for 2005 has been 
formulated based on provisions of the Law on the National Bank of 
Moldova and was subject to coordination with relevant economic and 
financial authorities of the State and approved by the Council of 
Administration of the National Bank of Moldova on 14 December 
2004.  
The macroeconomic framework applied for the elaboration of the 
Monetary and Foreign Exchange Policy of the National Bank of 
Moldova for 2005 included the Economic Development Scenarios of 
the Republic of Moldova under the Medium-Term Expenditure 
Framework for the years 2005 – 2007 (Decision of the Government of 
the Republic of Moldova no. 639 of 08 June 2004). The basic 
indicators of the Monetary and Foreign Exchange Policy of the 
National Bank of Moldova for 2005 provided as follows:  
• inflation rate within 8% to 10%; 
• real GDP growth by 6.0%. 
The monetary program for 2005 provided as follows: 
• increase of broad money M2 by 45.0%; 


Annual Report 2005                                                                                                                Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
28
• increase of reserve money by 41.0% as compared to 2004-end. 
GDP growth in 2005 accounted for 7.1% in real terms. As in 
accordance with preliminary data, the value of external trade with 
goods and services over 2005 enlarged by 22.9% as compared to the 
previous year; exports rose by 13.3%; and imports – by 29.1%.   
The positive dynamics of macroeconomic indicators, intensification of 
sterilisation operations by the National Bank of Moldova, as well as 
the tempering of non-monetary factors’ impact over 2005 permitted to 
enframe the inflation rate within set provisions of the Monetary and 
Foreign Exchange Policy for 
2005. 
Within this context, the growth 
rate of consumer prices over 
2005 followed the previously 
noted descendant trend and 
accounted, as of 2005-end, for 
10.0%, including of foodstuffs 
– for 8.7%; non-food products 
– for 14.9%; and services – for 
6.6%. Inflation rate in 2005 has 
been mainly affected by the 
consolidation 
of 
domestic 
demand following remittances’ 
inflow; augmentation of wage 
incomes of the population; as well as rises in fuel prices in the 
international market, which led to the increase of domestic prices for 
fuel by 35.7% and, ultimately, for passengers’ transportation by 
14.2%.  
To attenuate excessive fluctuations of the official exchange rate of the 
national currency against US$, the National Bank of Moldova 
intervened in the domestic forex market using US$ buying / selling 
operations and reverse swap (selling) transactions. Following 
interventions in the domestic forex market through acquisition of 
foreign currency surplus, NBM consolidated its international reserves 
and conducted national currency issuance.  
Thus, NBM acquisitions of foreign currency over 2005 totalled US$ 
174.8 million in net values, which determined international reserves’ 
enhancement by US$ 127.1 million (27.0%) up to US$ 597.4 million.  
At the same time, to attenuate relevant pressures of money issuance on 
price level following foreign currency acquisition, the National Bank 
intensified its sterilisation operations, which totalled US$ 8641.6 
million in 2005.

As result, reserve money expanded by 31.8% from 31 December 2004 
and stayed in line with the limits stipulated in the Monetary and 
Foreign Exchange Policy of the National Bank for 2005. The balance 
of currency in circulation increased by 871.3 million lei (23.5%); and 
overall banks’ reserves – by 819.2 million lei (50.8%). 
Broad money M2 (broad money in Moldovan Lei) consolidated by 
36.7% in 2005, including deposits in national currency – by 47.7%. 
Deposits in foreign currency expressed in US$ rose by 27.4% (as 
against 31.7% in 2004).  
The broad money M2 dynamics generated the increase of the 
monetization level up to 30.3% in December 2005 from 25.4% in 
December 2004. The upward trend of the monetization indicator 
illustrates the positive dynamics of financial intermediation in the 
national economy.        
  
Dynamics of Monetary Indicators  
Reserve Money 
Reserve money∗ in 2005 increased by 31.8% (1689.8 million lei) and 
amounted to 7002.9 million lei as of 31 December 2005. 
The 
amplification 
of 
sterilisation operations by the 
National Bank of Moldova in 
the forex market constituted 
the main factor that induced 
the enlargement of reserve 
money. Net foreign assets 
denoted noticeable increases 
over 2005, while net domestic 
assets reduced as result of 
intensified 
sterilisation 
operations (chart no. 25). 
The 50.8% rise of banks’ 
reserves over the year was 
mainly due to the larger value 
of 
means 
in 
commercial 
banks’ correspondent accounts with the National Bank of Moldova. 
Excessive banks’ reserves consolidated by 2.1 times and totalled 
1057.7 million lei as of 2005-end. 
                                                 
∗ Reserve money includes currency in circulation (outside the banking system), banks’ reserves (reserves of commercial banks in lei 
maintained in correspondent accounts with the National Bank of Moldova and currency in banks’ vaults) and sight deposits of other 
organisations with the National Bank of Moldova.

Such consolidation of banks’ excess reserves determined the reduction 
of liquidity coefficient from 0.83 in 2004 down to 0.70 in 2005, which 
led to the increase of monetary multiplier.  
The monetary multiplier rose over the year by 3.9% (from 1.53 up to 
1.59), illustrating the intensified financial intermediation and, 
ultimately, the more pronounced growth rate of broad money (M2) 
over reserve money.       
 
Broad Money Dynamics  
Broad Money M2 (broad money in MDL) increased over the year by 
2988.6 million lei, or by 36.7%. Broad money M3 rose by 4107.1 
million lei or by 35.0% and totalled 15826.8 million lei as of 31 
December 2005.  
The larger value of broad money M3 was due to the dynamics of 
implied components (chart no. 26), 
especially 
of 
the 
monetary 
aggregate 
M2 
(currency 
in 
circulation, deposits in MDL and 
money market instruments), which 
rose over the year by 36.7% (as 
against 44.7% in 2004). 
Within 
the 
structure 
of 
the 
monetary aggregate M2, the weight 
of currency in circulation (outside 
banks) reduced from 45.5% in 
2004 to 41.1% in 2005, while the 
weight of deposits in national 
currency (deposits of individuals 
and legal entities) enlarged from 
54.5% 
to 
58.9%.

more

of 
broad

population’s

which

Currency in circulation (M0) rose 
by 23.5% (871.3 million lei) and 
amounted to 4571.2 million lei.

of currency in circulation (1.8%)

Monetary policy instruments  
Interest Rate Policy    
 
In 2005 the National Bank of Moldova has further aimed at orienting 
interest rates in the money market within the interest rates’ corridor 
limited by the interest rate on overnight deposits and interest rate on 
overnight credits. The rate on 2-month State Securities buying 
REPOs, which is the NBM base rate, served as reference point in 
establishing the interest rates on NBM monetary instruments.    
The National Bank of Moldova has further promoted a prudent 
interest rate policy, establishing the interest rates based on inflation 
rate and general dynamics of other economic and financial indicators. 
Decisions on interest rates approved by NBM over 2005 are included 
in table no. 21.  
In 2005 the National Bank reduced the interest rates on instruments of    
monetary regulation twice. The base rate has been lowered by 2.0 
percentage points; the interest rate on overnight deposits – by 3.0 
percentage points; the interest rate on Lombard facility and overnight 
credits – by 2.0 percentage points (chart no. 36).

Liquidity Management Operations of the National Bank of Moldova    
In 2005 the National Bank of Moldova faced a 
permanent liquidity surplus in the money 
market determined, as well, by acquisitions of 
foreign currency. 
To compensate the impact of conducted 
interventions in the forex market upon price 
growth, the National Bank of Moldova 
intensified the sterilisation of excess liquidity. 
In 2005 the National Bank implemented a new 
instrument – NBM   Certificates, which in 
common with deposits’ attraction from banks, 
constituted the main 
instruments used to 
sterilise 
excess 
liquidity 
in 
the 
banking system. The 
volume of absorbing 
operations in 2005 
amounted to 8641.6 
million lei, by 3.1 
times more than in 
the 
previous 
year 
(chart no. 37).

Required Reserves     
The transit period to separate reserving in MDL and free convertible 
currencies (FCC) from means attracted in relevant currencies as 
established following the Decision of the Council of Administration 
of the National Bank of Moldova no. 114 of 20 May 2004 has been 
concluded at the end of the 1st quarter of 2005. 
Starting from the attracted resources’ observing period of 1 – 15 April 
2005, required reserves from means attracted in free convertible 
currency are integrally maintained in free convertible currency (US$ 
and Euro) and required reserves from means attracted in MDL and 
non-convertible currencies – in MDL.  
The value of monthly average required reserves in MDL formed by 
banks in December 2005 amounted to 775.5 million lei, surpassing by 
11.3% or 78.9 million lei the value registered in December 2004. 
Required reserves in US$ and Euro as of 2005-end totalled, 
respectively US$ 23.7 million and EUR 13.8 million (by US$ 11.5 
million or 94.3% and EUR 8.6 million or 2.7 times more than as of 
2004-end).  
The National Bank paid to banks distinct interest rates for required 
reserves maintained in MDL and free convertible currencies, applying 
the average interest rate on interest-bearing demand deposits attracted 
by the banking sector in MDL and FCC, calculated based on monthly 
reports submitted by banks.  
The annual average interest rate applied for MDL required reserves’ 
remuneration by NBM in 2005 accounted for 3.58% per year; and the 
interest rate applied on remuneration of required reserves in FCC – 
for 0.38% per year. NBM expenses on required reserves’ 
remuneration amounted to 12.64 million lei.       
 
 
 
 


Annual Report 2005                                                                                                                Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
40
 
Lending Activity  
 
Direct Credits to Banks     
In 2005 the National Bank has further monitored credits extended to 
banks over 1992 to 2002 for purposes of completion of housing co-
operative construction works.  
The balance of credits extended to banks reduced by 17.0% in 2005 – 
from 62.3 million lei as of 31 December 2004 to 51.7 million lei as of 
31 December 2005. Such dynamics was due to credit payments, 
especially to advance reimbursements. The volume of credit paid in 
advance totalled 9.1 million lei, or 85.8% of credit reimbursement in 
2005 (by 12.8 percentage points more than in 2004).     
  
Government Debt to the National Bank of Moldova   
 
In 2005 the National Bank of Moldova executed budgetary policy 
provisions based on the Law on the State Budget for 2005 no. 373-
XV of 11 November 2004, the Law on Modification of Article 27 of 
the Law on the State Budget for 2004 no. 195-XV of 17 June 2004, 
the Law on State Debt and State Guarantees no. 943-XIII of 18 July 
1996 and the Law on the National Bank of Moldova no. 548-XIII of 
21 July 1995.  
As of 31 December 2005, the Government debt to the National Bank 
of Moldova reduced by 4.6% from 2004-end following credit 
reimbursement of 127.0 million lei by the Ministry of Finance as 
provided in the Law on the State Budget for 2005. 
There is a positive downward trend of the share of Government debt 
to the National Bank of Moldova within total domestic public debt 
and the enlargement of debt share in form of state securities placed in 
the market. Thus, as of 2005-end, the Government debt to the 
National Bank of Moldova reduced to 68.9% of total debt (as 
compared to 73.7% as of 2004-end).

Annual Report 2005                                                                                                                Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
41
The Government debt to NBM as of 31 December 2005 amounted to 
2611.0 million lei and included the debt on NBM loans in a value of 
2211.0 million lei and the debt in form of SS in NBM portfolio in a 
value of 400.0 million lei (at purchasing price) (table no. 22).  
 
Lending Activity   
The National Bank of Moldova did not extend any new credit to the 
Government in 2005.  
Loans extended to the Government have been re-concluded for terms 
of up to 91 days at market interest rates, which evolved from 6.36% 
per year at the beginning of January to 3.77% per year at December-
end.    
 
State Securities in the NBM Portfolio  
The Government debt to the National Bank of Moldova in form of 
state securities in the NBM portfolio amounted to 400.0 million lei (at 
purchasing price) as of 31 December 2005 and did not registered any 
modification from 2004-end. 
Over 2005 the National Bank re-issued state securities from own 
portfolio for terms of 91 days and 98 days. Interest rates on issued 
securities varied within 5.24% per year in January to 3.74% per year 
in December. 
As a result of reflected accrued interest rates and evaluation of state 
securities in NBM portfolio at market prices, the balance sheet value 
of Government debt as of 31 December 2005 amounted to 2625.9 
million lei, including:  
- debt on loans to the Government – 2223.0 million lei; 
- debt in form of SS in NBM portfolio – 402.9 million lei.         
 
 
Money Market   
 
Primary Market of State Securities   
Acting as fiscal agent of the State in performing selling, recording and 
redemption of state securities, the National Bank of Moldova 
conducted in 2005 176 issues of state securities aimed at budgetary 
deficit financing.

Interbank Money Market   
The value of transactions in the interbank 
market in 2005 totalled 10312.3 million lei, augmenting by 3738.9 
million lei or 57.0% from 2004.  
Banks’ transactions with the National Bank of Moldova in 2005 
accounted for 83.8% of total turnover in the interbank market, 
amounting to 8641.6 million lei. 
Interbank credits / deposits accounted for the largest share of 
interbank transactions and totalled 1604.6 million lei (96.0% of total 
turnover); REPOs valued 66.1 million lei (4.0%).  
The average weighted interest rates on interbank transactions 
(excluding NBM) over 2005 evolved from 10.4% per year in January 
to 6.54% per year at 2005-end. 
Transactions concluded between banks implied longer terms; the 
average weighted term accounted for around 9 days, expanding by 3 
days from 2004 due to the reduced weight of overnight transactions in 
the total annual turnover (from 85.0% in 2004 to 69.0% in 2005).

45
To further improve the interbank market condition and duly 
implement international standards and practices, the National Bank of 
Moldova initiated the process of determining the reference rates in the 
interbank market of the Republic of Moldova CHIBOR and CHIBID. 
The CHIBOR and CHIBID reference rates are calculated, starting 
with 1 December 2005, by REUTERS based on relevant quotations 
displayed by banks selected by the National Bank of Moldova and are 
daily published on the web page of the National Bank of Moldova.        
 
Secondary Market of State Securities   
In 2005 the secondary market of state securities (except open market 
operations of the National Bank of Moldova) narrowed its activity on 
the background of maintained excess liquidity in the money market, as 
well as the continuous descendant dynamics of securities’ yields.  
The number of selling / buying transactions with state securities in the 
secondary market reduced by 4.2 times from the previous year. The 
value of these transactions amounted to 
147.7 million lei, decreasing almost twice 
from 2004 (chart no. 47). 
The value of transaction within the 
segment of state securities maturing 
under 1 year amounted to 145.7 million 
lei, materially decreasing by 162.6 
million lei or by almost 2.1 times from 
2004. 
Transactions with State Bonds totalled 
2.0 million lei and have been conducted 
at an average weighted effective interest 
rate of 5.46% for an average weighted 
term of 726 days till maturity.  
Within the structure of state securities 
commercialised in the secondary market, 
28-day state securities, as well as 
securities with terms of 28 days to 91 
days, were most attractive to market 
makers, accounting, respectively, for 
38.9% and 32.4% of total transactions 
with state securities (chart no. 48). The 
share of securities with terms longer than 
273 days consolidated from 2.7% in 2004 
to 26.0% in 2005.

State Securities Recording in the Book Entry System of the National 
Bank of Moldova (BES) 
As of 31 December 2005, state securities registered in the Book Entry 
System of the National Bank of Moldova amounted to 3988.6 million 
lei in nominal terms. In breakdown by issuer, the relevant structure is 
as follows: 
I. Ministry of Finance – SS in total amount of 3846.9 million lei, 
including:    
- 1207.7 million lei or 31.4% - SS issued through primary market 
auctions;   
- 2234.7 million lei or 58.1% - SS issued and delivered to the 
National Bank of Moldova to guarantee loans extended to the State; 
- 404.5 million lei or 10.5% - SS issued through conversion of loans 
previously extended to the State. 
II. National Bank of Moldova – NBM Certificates in total value of 
141.7 million lei, issued through primary market auctions. 
The total value of SS registered in BES at nominal value in 
breakdown by holder is as follows:

As of 31 December 2005, the weight of state securities held by the 
Ministry of Finance and NBM within the structure of SS registered in 
BES by holder, broadened from 2004-end following the use by the 
National Bank of Moldova at the end of 2004 of state securities within 
open market operations (chart no. 49).  
NBM Certificates totalling 141.7 million lei in nominal terms have 
been registered in BES integrally in portfolios of commercial banks. 
BES of the National Bank of Moldova registered over the reported 
year 4844 transactions amounting to 35842.3 million lei (by 4149.7 
million lei more than in 2004), including: 
1. primary market operations (new issues) – 17262.4 million lei; 
2. repurchase by the Ministry of Finance of state securities upon 
maturity - 13079.0 million lei; 
3. repurchase by the National Bank of Moldova of NBM Certificates 
upon maturity - 4028.4 million lei; 
4. open market operations with state securities conducted by NBM - 
313.5 million lei; 
5. selling / buying transactions - 155.7 million lei; 
6. interbank REPOs (stage I and II) – 136.6 million lei; 
7. pledge operations – 866.7 million lei.

The 11 primary Dealers active in the state securities market in 2005 
supported the relevant and adequate performance of primary market 
placement auctions of state securities and the secondary market 
liquidity.

Annual Report 2005                                                                                                                Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
48
With the view to establishing optimal conditions for banks acting as 
primary Dealers, the Council of Administration of the National Bank 
of Moldova approved on 10 March 2005 more flexible criteria related 
to primary Dealers’ activity in the state securities market as follows:  
1. Primary Dealers that hold assets in a value larger or equal to 800.0 
million lei should as follows: 
• participate at every state securities selling auction; 
• acquire within one quarter SS at nominal value in a minimum 
quantum of 2.0% of the average quarterly offer announced by 
the Ministry of Finance over the previous four quarters. 
2. Primary Dealers that hold assets in a value under 800.0 million lei 
should as follows: 
• participate at minimum 50.0% of total state securities selling 
auctions conducted over one month; 
• acquire within one quarter SS at nominal value in a minimum 
quantum of 1.0% of the average quarterly offer announced by 
the Ministry of Finance over the previous four quarters. 
3. Primary Dealers should conclude transactions with state securities 
in the secondary market in a value of 0.3% of total transactions 
conducted by all primary Dealers over the previous quarter.  
In 2005, primary Dealers have, based on their right to buy state 
securities both on own name and account and on the name and 
account of investors (their clients) – individuals and legal entities, 
residents and non-residents of the Republic of Moldova, placed bids at 
state securities selling auctions in a total value of 4284.4 million lei, 
surpassing by 1945.4 million the offer announced by the Ministry of 
Finance.  
The value of state securities bought by primary Dealers over 2005 
amounted to 2195.4 million lei, which accounts for 93.9% of the offer 
by the Ministry of Finance (93.7% in 2004).  
The National Bank absorbed 8520.0 million lei through primary 
Dealers, including as follows:  
• 4150.8 million lei through placement of Certificates of the 
National Bank;  
• 4369.2 million lei through deposits’ attraction.  
As in accordance with provisions of the Agreement of Execution of 
Primary Dealer Functions in the Market of Dematerialised State 
Securities concluded between the National Bank of Moldova and 
banks – primary Dealers, primary Dealers assured daily quotations of 
state securities through the “interbank messages” system.

49
Trends in the Foreign Exchange Market  
Exchange Rate Trends in 2005  
The official nominal exchange rate of the national currency against 
US$ depreciated over the year by 3.0% (from 12.4600 MDL per US$ 
1 as of 31 December 2004 down to 12.8320 MDL per US$ 1 as of 31 
December 2005); and against Euro appreciated by 10.4% (from 
16.9542 MDL per Euro 1 as of 31 December 2004 up to 15.1950 
MDL per Euro 1 as of 31 December 2005). 
The dynamics of the average exchange 
rate of the national currency against 
US$ in the domestic cash forex market 
over the reported year denoted similar 
trends to that of the official exchange 
rate of the national currency against 
US$, except for more pronounced 
deviations marked by appreciation 
trends at the beginning of May 2005 
and depreciation trends in the second 
half of November 2005. 
The official average exchange rate of 
the national currency against US$ over 
2005 amounted to 12.6003 MDL per 
US$ 1, as compared to 12.3283 MDL per US$ 1 in 2004. The official 
average exchange rate of the national currency against Euro totalled 
15.6978 MDL per Euro 1, as compared to 15.3286 MDL per Euro 1 in 
2004.  
The dynamics of the average exchange rate of the national currency 
against US$ in the domestic forex market over 2005 has been mainly 
affected by the following factors: 
1) Seasonal character of foreign currency net flows in the domestic 
forex market  
Foreign currency inflows and outflows into / 
from the national economy, as well as the 
cyclic character of the national economic 
activity represent the main factors that 
affected the dynamics of the exchange rate 
over 2005. Chart no. 51 displays the 
seasonal character of the net foreign 
currency flow generated from foreign 
currency 
inflows 
and 
outflows. 
The 
depreciation of the national currency against

50
US$ in the 1st and 4th quarters of 2005 occurred on the background of 
insignificant positive net flows of US$ 14.2 million and US$ 16.8 
million, respectively. The largest depreciation of the national currency 
against US$ - by 1.7%, determined by the maximal net flow of US$ 
20.3 million, has been recorded in November 2005. 
In the 2nd and 3rd quarters of 2005, backed by a significant flow of 
US$ 113.4 million, the national currency appreciated against US$ by 
0.2%. The more noticeable appreciation of MDL against US$ over 
this period has been tempered by NBM significant interventions in the 
domestic forex market.  
2) Tempering of the growth rate of net foreign currency supply by 
individuals 
Another factor that impacted the dynamics of 
the official nominal exchange rate of the 
national currency against US$ is the double 
decline of the growth rate of foreign currency 
net supply by individuals – from 72.2% in 
2004 against 2003 down to 36.1% in 2005 
against 2004. 
In absolute values, the net supply of foreign 
currency by individuals still denotes weighty 
values, amounting to US$ 1039.3 million, or 
35.6% of GDP in 2005, as compared to 29.4% 
of GDP in 2004. The foreign currency net 
supply by individuals over the reported year 
surpassed by US$ 26.4 million the deficit of 
the balance with goods and services, as compared to US$ 106.0 
million in 2004.  
3) Appreciation trends of US$ against Euro in the international 
forex market   
The exchange rate of US$ against 
Euro in the international markets 
appreciated by 13.0% over 2005 – 
from US$ 1.3607 per Euro 1 as of 31 
December 2004 to US$ 1.1841 per 
Euro 1 as of 31 December 2005 
(chart no. 53).  
US$ deposits in 2005 enlarged by 
23.5% or US$ 41.7 million as 
compared to the previous year (by

4) Enhancement of net assets in banks’ balance sheet, as well as of

The depreciation trend of the official nominal exchange rate of the 
national currency against US$ in 2005 is also due to the expansion by 
26.4% of banks’ foreign currency assets and by 31.5% of foreign 
currency deposits by individuals, which 
generated the reduction of total foreign 
currency supply in the domestic forex 
market.  
Banks’ net foreign currency balance sheet 
assets rose over 2005 by US$ 105.5 
million (from US$ 399.7 million as of 31 
December 2004 to US$ 505.2 million as of 
31 December 2005), surpassing the 
relevant 
US$ 
73.6 
million-increase 
recorded in 2004. The most critical net 
foreign currency flow of US$ 38.9 million 
has been observed in August 2005 and 
banks expanded net balance sheet assets 
by US$ 41.7 million, thus assisting the tempering of the national 
currency appreciation against US$. Foreign currency deposits by 
individuals rose over 2005 by US$ 63.5 million.

Foreign exchange inflow in 2005 amounted to US$ 2632.8 million 
and included as follows: receipts from exports of goods and services – 
US$ 1343.3 million (51.0%); net foreign currency supply by 
individuals – US$ 1039.3 million (39.5%); external credits – US$ 
152.5 million (5.8%); net direct and portfolio investments – US$ 97.7 
million (3.7%).    
Foreign exchange inflow over the reported period enlarged by US$ 
544.4 million (26.1%) as against the similar period of 2004. Such 
enlargement was mainly due to the amplification of foreign currency 
supply by individuals – by US$ 275.4 million (36.1%); receipts from 
exports of goods and services – by US$ 189.1 million (16.4%); and 
direct and portfolio investments – by US$ 62.7million (2.8 times). 
Charts no. 55 and no. 56 provides the dynamics of the average 
monthly exchange rate against US$ and of foreign currency inflows 
and outflows over 2004 to 2005.      
Foreign exchange outflow in 2005 totalled US$ 2488.4 million, 
determined by payments on imports of goods and services (US$ 
2356.2 million or 94.7%); and on external debt servicing (US$ 132.2 
million or 5.3%).  
Foreign exchange outflow intensified over 2005 by US$ 575.2 million 
(30.1%), determined by the increase by US$ 544.1 million (30.0%) of 
payments on imports of goods and services and by US$ 31.1 million 
(30.8%) of external credits’ servicing.

The competitiveness index computed for the CIS zone has, after 
reaching the highest level of 107.3% in 2000, 
continuously decreased to 57.8% in 2005. The 
competitiveness index computed for EU countries 
reduced from 52.7% in 2004 down to 45.0% in 
2005, mainly as result of more pronounced growth 
of imports from the European Union (by 32.2%) 
and smaller enlargement of exports to this region 
(by 12.9%). The lowest competitiveness index of 
29.6% has been registered for countries of Central 
and Eastern Europe, which was due to the 
considerable surpassing of imports over exports 
related to this region.  
   
Foreign Exchange Market of the Republic of Moldova  
The foreign exchange market of the Republic of Moldova over 2005 
exhibited ascendant trends of transactions conducted in the domestic 
forex market against MDL due to the intensified activity of residents, 
as well as of non-residents. The total turnover of foreign exchange 
traded against MDL over the reported year totalled the equivalent of 
US$ 6251.5∗∗ million, increasing by 23.3% from 2004 (table no. 25). 
Within total turnover, purchase of foreign exchange in the domestic 
forex market against MDL amounted to US$ 3142.0 million, and sales 
–to US$ 3109.5 million.

Annual Report  2005                                                                                                             Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
55
The volume of transactions in the domestic forex market over 2005 
displayed ascendant trends, reaching the maximal value in the 3rd 
quarter. The turnover of transfer transactions in the forex market rose 
by 21.5% as compared to 2004, and that of cash transactions – by 
28.3%. The weight of transfer transactions in the total turnover of 
transactions in the domestic forex market diminished by 1.1 
percentage point (from 73.4% in 2004 down to 72.3% in 2005); the 
weight of cash transactions rose by 1.1 percentage point (from 26.6% 
in 2004 up to 27.7% in 2005). As compared to 2004, the structure of  
the total turnover of transactions concluded between the main forex 
market participants over 2005 modified as follows: the weight of 
transactions conducted by non-resident banks extended by 3.6 
percentage points; by exchange bureaus of commercial banks – by 1.0 
percentage point; by non-bank exchange offices – by 0.1 percentage 
point; while the weight of transactions by the National Bank of 
Moldova reduced by 3.8 percentage points; by legal entities – by 1.2 
percentage point; and by resident banks – by 0.3 percentage point.  
The turnover of transactions by exchange bureaus of commercial 
banks in 2005 accounted for 22.3% of total turnover of transactions in 
the domestic forex market. The weight of transactions’ turnover by 
non-bank exchange offices reduced by 4.1 times.  
The intensified turnover of forex transactions over 2005 was 
determined by the amplification of foreign exchange transactions 
related to imports / exports of goods and services by economic agents 
of the Republic of Moldova conducted through resident commercial 
banks; the augmented value of remittances from abroad; transactions 
with non-resident banks; as well as disbursement and servicing of 
external credits.  
The foreign exchange market liquidity∗ in 2005 rose from 0.77% in 
2004 up to 0.84% in 2005. The components of this index, especially 
the average daily turnover of transactions in 
foreign currency against MDL and GDP 
increased, as compared to the previous year, by 
respectively US$ 4.6 million (from US$ 20.0 
million to US$ 24.6 million) and by US$ 322.0 
million (from US$ 2595.0 million to US$ 
2917.0 million). The maximal values of forex 
market liquidity have been noted in the 3rd 
quarter, especially in August and September 
(1.04%) (chart no. 60).  
                                                 
∗ Represents the ratio between the average daily turnover of transactions against MDL and GDP (in %)

Annual Report  2005                                                                                                             Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
56
As compared to neighbouring countries and 
the main trade partners of the Republic of 
Moldova, the forex market liquidity of the 
Republic of Moldova is lower than in Russia 
(2.82%) and higher than in Ukraine (0.68%) 
and in Belarus (0.50%) (table no. 26). 
With the view to attenuate excessive 
fluctuations of the official nominal exchange 
rate of the national currency against US$, 
over 2005 the National Bank of Moldova 
intervened in the domestic forex market 
using the following instruments: US$ selling 
/ buying transactions in a net value of US$ 
171.4 million (including forward transactions 
in an amount of US$ 7.9 million) and reverse 
swap (selling) transactions in a value of US$ 
15.03 million (chart no. 61). 
Payments under external public debt servicing 
(including servicing of IMF credits) totalled 
US$ 76.3 million in 2005. 
International reserves enlarged over 2005 by 
27.0% from US$ 470.3 million up to US$ 
597.4 million∗. To the end of the year, 
international reserves expressed in import-
months reached the level of  2.6 months∗∗. Of 
special importance is the noticeable liquidity 
ratio of international reserves∗∗∗, which 
accounted for 491.8% in 2005, increasing by 
240.7 percentage points as compared to the 
previous year (chart no. 62).  
The average annual shares of foreign 
currencies in the total foreign exchange 
turnover exhibited the following values: 
transactions in US$ accounted for 71.5%; in 
Euro – for 24.3%; in Russian Roubles – for 
3.7%; and in other currencies – for 0.5% 
(table no. 27).

57
US$ is still prevailing in forex market transactions of the Republic of 
Moldova. Yet, over 2005, the share of US$ transactions in Moldovan 
forex market reduced by 4.2 percentage points; while the share of 
Euro transactions rose by 3.7 percentage points. In 2005 the weight of 
Euro purchases in the cash forex market increased by 9.7 percentage 
points (from 39.8% in 2004 to 49.5 in 2005); and of Euro sales – by 
5.1 percentage points (from 30.0% to 35.1%). The larger share of 
Euro transactions in the cash forex market is due to the increased 
value of Euro transfers by citizens of the Republic of Moldova 
working abroad. 
As compared to 2004, foreign exchange purchases by commercial 
banks of the Republic of Moldova in the domestic forex market over 
2005 rose by US$ 582.5 million (24.4%). Such increase was mainly 
due to larger foreign currency acquisitions from individuals (by US$ 
301.1 million or 31.9%) and from foreign banks (by US$ 144.2 
million or 122.5%).

Annual Report  2005                                                                                                             Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
58
Over 2005 banks conducted purchases of foreign currency in the 
domestic forex market mainly from individuals (41.9%); from legal 
entities (37.9%); from non-resident banks (8.8%); and from resident 
banks (6.8%). 
As compared to 2004, over the reported year the weight of foreign 
currency acquisition from individuals rose by 2.4 percentage points; 
and from non-resident banks – by 3.9 percentage points. At the same 
time decreases have been noted in the weights of foreign currency 
purchases from legal entities - by 5.8 percentage points; and from 
NBM – by 1.2 percentage point. These modifications led to the 
reduction of the common weight of legal entities and individuals as 
source of foreign currency acquisition by 3.4 percentage points - from 
83.2% in 2004 to 79.8% in 2005. 
Foreign exchange sales by commercial banks of the Republic of 
Moldova in the domestic forex market over 2005 rose by US$ 530.6 
million, or by 22.0%, mainly due to larger sales of foreign exchange 
towards legal entities (by US$ 455.7 million or 28.9%) and non-
resident banks (by US$ 140.8 million or 113.9%). Commercial banks’ 
sales of foreign currency in the domestic forex market over 2005 to 
legal entities accounted for 69.1%; to non-resident banks – for 9.0%, 
to individuals – for 7.1%; to resident banks – for 6.9%; and to NBM – 
for 6.6%.  
As compared to 2004, increased shares have been noted for sales of 
foreign currency to non-resident banks (by 3.9 percentage points); and 
to legal entities (by 3.7 percentage points), and reduced shares – for 
sales to the National Bank of Moldova (by 6.8 percentage points); to

Dynamics of Banks’ Assets and Liabilities in Foreign Currency∗ 
As of 31 December 2005 the net balance sheet assets in foreign 
currency of commercial banks (extended credits, available funds, 
required reserves and other assets in foreign currency) rose as from 31 
December 2004 by US$ 105.5 million (from US$ 399.7 million up to 
US$ 505.2 million), or by 26.4% (table no. 31).  
Within total assets in foreign currency, credits in foreign currency 
accounted for the largest share of 58.3% as of 31 December 2005 and 
varied within 55.4% to 66.0%. As compared to 2004-end, the balance 
of credits extended in foreign currency as of 31 December 2005 rose by

Significant increase over the reported 
year has been observed within US$ 
available funds. Thus, the balance of 
funds in US$ enlarged by 48.9% or by 
US$ 30.6 million (from US$ 62.6 million 
as of 31 December 2004 to US$ 93.2 
million as of 31 December 2005). As of 
2005-end, the share of US$ funds in total 
available funds in foreign currency 
expanded by 3.9 percentage points as 
compared to 2004-end (from 54.7% to 
58.6%).  
The balance of Euro funds as of 2005-end increased from 2004-end by 
15.7% and by the equivalent of US$ 7.0 million in terms of absolute 
values (from US$ 44.7 million to US$ 51.7 million). Yet, in share 
terms, Euro funds in total available funds in foreign currency 
diminished over the reported year by 6.6 percentage points (from 
39.1% to 32.5%). 
Required reserves in total net balance sheet assets in foreign currency 
rose by 2.1 times or by US$ 20.8 million (from US$ 19.3 million as of 
31 December 2004 to US$ 40.1 million as of 31 December 2005). The 
share of required reserves in total net balance sheet assets in foreign 
currency accounted for 7.9% as of 31 December 2005 and varied over 
the year from 4.1% to 8.6%.    
Net balance-sheet liabilities in foreign currency over the reported year 
increased by 25.3% or by US$ 96.9 million (from US$ 382.7 million as 
of 31 December 2004 to US$ 479.6 million as of 31 December 2005) 
(table no. 35).  
Clients’ time deposits in foreign currency accounted for the largest 
share of 51.5% of total net balance sheet liabilities. The relevant 
balance as of 2005-end rose by US$ 68.3 million from 2004-end (from 
US$ 178.5 million to US$ 246.8 million), or by 38.3%.

Clients’ sight deposits in foreign currency accounted for 27.4% of total 
net balance sheet liabilities in foreign currency as of the end of reported 
period. The balance of these deposits augmented over 2005 by US$ 
14.8 million in absolute values (from US$ 116.5 million as of 31 
December 2004 to US$ 131.3 million as of 31 December 2005), or by 
12.7%.  
The weight of credits received in foreign currency in total net balance 
sheet liabilities accounted for 9.9% as of 31 December 2005, and 
reduced by US$ 8.5 million from end of 2005 (from US$ 55.8 million 
to US$ 47.3 million), or by 15.2%.  
Banks’ time deposits in foreign 
currency abroad increased by US$ 
3.3 million (from US$ 12.2 million as 
of 31 December 2004 to US$ 15.5

by 27.0%. The share of these deposits 
in total net balance sheet liabilities in

enlarged over the reported period by

balance sheet liabilities in foreign currency accounted for 2.2% as of

Basic balance sheet liabilities in foreign currency (clients’ time 
deposits, sight deposits) increased by US$ 83.1 million (from US$

Foreign Exchange Regulation in 2005  
 
Over 2005 the National Bank of Moldova has further improved the 
legal framework for foreign exchange regulation within the context of 
gradual liberalisation of foreign exchange rules, as well as paying due 
consideration to relevant modifications within the effective legislation 
of the Republic of Moldova. 
• 
Given the modification of the Law on Regulation of Repatriation 
of Money Means, Goods and Services Obtained from External Trade 
Transactions (no. 1466-XIII of 29.01.1998), under which the National 
Bank of Moldova (NBM) and commercial banks have been 
exonerated from performance of repatriation control, a series of NBM 
normative acts in the field of foreign exchange regulation have been 
modified, and certain acts have been abrogated.      
• As in accordance with the Law on Revision and Improvement of 
the Normative Framework for Entrepreneurial Activity Regulation 
(no. 424-XV of 16 December 2004), a series of NBM normative acts 
in the field of foreign exchange regulation have been modified as to 
exclude provisions allowing NBM to request other documents not 
explicitly specified in these acts. At the same time, other normative 
acts clearly stipulate the documents that used to be additionally 
requested for purposes of examination of authorisation issuance 
requests. 
• Foreign exchange provisions regulating the activity of duty-free 
shops with foreign currency have been adjusted paying due 
consideration to the following legislative provisions of the Republic of 
Moldova:  
a) stipulation of duty-free shops’ right to trade goods against MDL, 
as well, in addition to trading in foreign currency; 
b) determination that duty-free shops do not represent customs 
regime, but customs destination of goods’ placement; 
c) abrogation of the Decree of the President of the Republic of 
Moldova no. 195 of 10 June 1994 on Organization of Shops 


Annual Report  2005                                                                                                             Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
67
Trading Goods under Duty-Free Regime, as well as of Shops 
Serving Diplomatic Corps; 
At the same time, the list of documents to be submitted for purposes 
of receiving relevant authorisation to trade goods against foreign 
currency in duty-free shops has been updated; and the term provided 
for the examination of requests for relevant authorisation issuance has 
been reduced. 
• Within regulation of registration / authorisation by NBM of private 
external commitments, NBM undertook the following actions. 
Following the modifications within the Law on Regulation of 
Repatriation of Money Means, Goods and Services Obtained from 
External Trade Transactions (no. 1466-XIII of 29.01.1998), with 
regard to the modification of repatriation terms (prolongation of 
thereof up to one year and longer, depending on the case), as well as 
the liberalisation of a series of operations related to capital export, 
NBM excluded the requirement on authorisation of international 
credit loan / credit extended by residents to non-residents for terms 
longer than 180 days and smaller than 365 days.  
Stipulations establishing additional requirements based on which 
NBM used to take the decision to authorise credit / loan, or financial 
leasing provision by residents (other than financial institutions) to 
non-residents have been abolished, as well.  
At the same time, it has been established that residents should register 
their private external commitments at NBM prior the use of money 
funds as in accordance with contracts on private external 
commitments. In accordance with previous provisions, private 
external commitments were registered at NBM before the execution 
of relevant contacts.  
• Considering effective legislative provisions, the norms within the 
foreign exchange domain related to issuance of authorisations to 
conduct certain foreign currency transactions by economic agents 
acting within civil aviation have been modified as for such provisions 
to cover all international airports of the Republic of Moldova 
determined so by the legislation. 
NBM has simplified, as well, the procedure related to the submission 
of documents for purposes of obtaining the NBM authorisation to 
conduct certain operations in foreign currency by economic agents 
acting within civil aviation; and also reduced the list of documents to 
be submitted for the examination by NBM of relevant authorisation 
issuance request. 


Annual Report  2005                                                                                                             Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
68
Additionally, NBM provided stipulations related to the examination 
term by NBM of new authorisation issuance requests in events set by 
effective normative acts. 
• Within the context of cash exchange activity with individuals, 
considering the Law on Licensing of Certain Types of Activity (no. 
451-XV of 30 July 2001), NBM prolonged the term set for the 
submission by relevant exchange offices to NBM of adequate 
explanations for violations traced out following conducted controls, 
as well as the term set for the NBM sanctioning decision. 
Under the scope of the Law on Financial Institutions (no. 550-XIII of 
21 July 1995), NBM set separate norms related to the application by 
NBM of sanctions towards commercial banks in events of non-
observance by thereof of normative acts related to cash exchange 
transactions with individuals.  
NBM modified, as well, the procedure of sanctions’ application in 
events of violation of effective legislative provisions regulating the 
use of cash and control equipment by exchange offices. To mention, 
effective legislative acts include provisions related to all categories of 
economic agents. These provisions cover issues related to violation of 
stipulations on use of cash and control equipment; measures 
undertaken towards offenders; as well as relevant entities authorised 
to apply sanctions (excluding NBM). Furtheron, it has been 
established that NBM shall not apply sanctions in events of violation 
of NBM normative acts related to use by exchange offices of cash and 
control equipment, and that NBM shall deliver the information on 
such violation to relevant entities for purposes of application of 
adequate measures as in accordance with the legislation in effect. 
• NBM modified over the reported year relevant foreign exchange 
regulations on card transactions.  
Along with the modifications in the repatriation legislation, NBM 
abolished the requirement of payment card application within 
repatriation payments and control, thus allowing resident legal entities 
to use cards not only for purposes of paying business trip related 
expenses abroad, but also to make other payments in favour of non-
residents. It is not allowed to withdraw cash through relevant cards in 
order to conduct such payments. NBM established the procedure of 
submitting to the bank adequate documents confirming the need of 
card payments, as well as the term of mentioned documents’ 
submission by resident legal entities. 
• Considering the perspectives of conducting payments / transfers 
based on documents bearing electronic signature, as well as the 


Annual Report  2005                                                                                                             Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
69
modification of repatriation control procedure, NBM modified the 
procedure of submission by resident individuals and legal entities of 
documents confirming the need of payment / transfer performance in 
favour of non-residents; of handling of these documents; and of 
keeping of thereof in the bank.  
In the event that the NBM normative acts in the field of foreign 
exchange regulation establish the need of submission by resident legal 
entities of documents confirming the need of payment / transfer 
performance and do not provide relevant rules on these documents’ 
submission, such submission shall fall under the scope of the special 
normative act approved in 2005 – the Instruction on Confirming 
Documents within Payments / Transfers by Resident Legal Entities in 
Favour of Non-Residents.  
This Instruction simplifies the rules on confirming documents’ 
submission and handling of thereof, including as follows:  
a) within performance of payments / transfers in favour of non-
residents, resident legal entities shall submit to the bank the 
confirming documents’ photocopies, the authenticity of which 
shall be certified by the relevant legal officer;  
b) the bank shall not mark the amount and date of conducted payment 
/ transfer on the photocopies of confirming documents, etc; 
c) confirming documents shall be submitted only once in the event 
that a number of payments is conducted based on the same 
confirming documents. 
Similar rules on confirming documents’ submission by resident legal 
entities and handling of thereof have been established for resident 
individuals.  
• Within the context of regulating the opening of residents’ accounts 
abroad, NBM conducted the followings. 
Considering that different rules and requirements are applied for 
different residents, Moldova’s residents have been divided into a 
number 
of 
categories. 
These 
amendments 
implied 
relevant 
specifications as with regard to documents to be submitted to NBM 
by the resident individual practicing certain type of activity for 
purposes of receiving relevant authorisation, as well as to the 
repatriation of balances of accounts closed abroad by this category of 
residents. 
At the same time, relevant regulations have been added with new 
provisions as follows: 


Annual Report  2005                                                                                                             Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
70
a) performance through accounts opened abroad of transactions 
related to capital transfer subject to registration / authorisation by 
NBM; 
b) operations not specified in the NBM authorisation that may be 
conducted through accounts opened abroad; 
c) documents to be submitted to NBM by relevant residents with the 
first report. 
Simultaneously, NBM simplified the list of documents to be delivered 
to NBM for purposes of obtaining the authorisation to open accounts 
abroad.  
Over 2005 NBM has approved, as well, other foreign exchange 
regulations, including as follows.  
• Banks have been allowed to extend foreign currency credits from 
own means for purposes of reimbursement of foreign currency credits 
received by residents from other banks of the Republic of Moldova, 
exclusively providing that such credit has been received to conduct 
payments with non-residents; 
• Within measures aimed at easing the submission of confirming 
documents and handling of thereof in event of payments / transfers in 
favour of non-residents, NBM has as follows:   
a) set similar provisions on confirming documents’ submission and 
maintenance in events of foreign currency acquisition against 
MDL by resident legal entities, paying due consideration that same 
confirming documents, in certain cases, serve as basis for 
performance of both relevant acquisitions, as well as payments / 
transfers in favour of non-residents;  
b)  adjusted the provision of the Instruction on International Money 
Orders to new rules, taking into account that the Instruction has 
been worked out based on norms related to transfers abroad via the 
banking system. 
• Improving foreign exchange normative acts, NBM has paid 
consideration to legislative provisions on assignment of state 
identifications numbers to enterprises and organizations, as well as on 
the introduction of the new certificate of enterprises and organizations 
registration.  
• Within the context of foreign currency import / export into / from 
the Republic of Moldova, it has been allowed to use, along with the 
customs declaration of foreign currency import, the permission to 
export foreign currency upon foreign currency cash / traveller’s check 
depositing in accounts of resident legal entities by non-residents (or 


Annual Report  2005                                                                                                             Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
71
authorised persons of thereof) in cases provided by relevant foreign 
exchange regulations. 
• The procedure related to MDL cash depositing by representative 
offices of non-resident legal entities into MDL accounts of thereof has 
been simplified.  
• With the view to ensuring due transparency of certain provisions 
regulating cash import / export into / from the Republic of Moldova 
by banks, NBM established the relevant time-framework provided for 
NBM decision on issuance of cash import / export authorisation in 
favour of non-resident bank or refusal of such authorisation issuance, 
as well as additional grounds based on which NBM may refuse to 
issue requested authorisation.  
• Considering the modifications and completions within the Chart of 
Accounts of Bookkeeping in Banks and Other Financial Institutions 
of the Republic of Moldova, as well as with the view to improving the 
system of statistic data collection, NBM modified in 2005 a series of 
regulations related to banks’ reporting to NBM.  
• Within the context of bringing the foreign exchange legislation of 
the Republic of Moldova in line with the European Union legislation, 
the National Bank of Moldova has further worked in 2005 on the 
elaboration of the draft Law on Foreign Exchange Regulation. Over 
2005 NBM benefited from relevant technical assistance and 
undertook adequate measures aimed at adjusting the NBM legal and 
operational framework for NBM independence consolidation and 
institution of price stability objective as basic objectives of the 
National Bank of Moldova.                
    
      
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


Annual Report  2005                                                                                                             Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
72
International Cooperation of the Republic of Moldova   
 
International Monetary Fund (IMF) 
The Republic of Moldova became member of the International 
Monetary Fund (IMF) on 12 August 1992. As of 2005-end, the 
subscription quota of the Republic of Moldova in IMF amounted to 
SDR 123.2 million (approximately US$ 176.1 million).  
The relationship of the National Bank of Moldova with IMF over 2005 
embodies monitoring of country’s macroeconomic and financial 
condition, as well as annual bilateral Article IV consultation missions.  
The financial support extended to the Republic of Moldova over 1992 
to 2005 totalled US$ 386.0 million (SDR 270.1 million): total 
reimbursements amounted to US$ 290.5 million (SDR 203.3 million), 
and interests and commissions – to US$ 85.2 million (SDR 59.6 
million). 
Moldova’s debt to IMF as at the end of 2005 totalled US$ 95.4 million 
(SDR 66.8 million). Total payments related to IMF credits’ servicing in 
2005 valued US$ 24.3 million (SDR 16.4 million), of which payments 
on credit principal amounted to US$ 21.6 million (SDR 14.6 million) 
and interest related payments – to US$ 2.7 million (SDR 1.8 million).  
Within the context of relationship of the Republic of Moldova with 
IMF, on 7 February 2005 the Executive Board of the International 
Monetary Fund approved the IMF experts’ Report following 
conclusion of Article IV consultation mission. IMF Directors 
recommended that within monetary policy implementation the National 
Bank of Moldova should pay special consideration to inflation target 
and ensurance of NBM independence, which shall further contribute to 
low-inflation maintenance. The Report appraised the favourable level 
of financial sector indicators.
