Annual Report  2005                                                                                                             Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
73
Within the four visits conducted over the reported year, the IMF 
experts assessed the macroeconomic performances; discussed with 
official executives of the Government of the Republic of Moldova and 
the National Bank of Moldova on issues of monetary and fiscal policies 
for the period of 2005 to 2006, implementation of structural reforms 
provided in the Poverty Reduction and Economic Growth Strategy and 
the Actions Plan Republic of Moldova – EU; as well as conducted the 
annual bilateral Article IV consultation for 2005. The 2005 December 
mission negotiated the basic components of a new macroeconomic 
program financed by IMF under the Poverty Reduction and Economic 
Growth Facility (PRGF). 
The IMF officials gave a positive appreciation of the NBM effort to 
maintain inflation at a low level and to sterilise excess liquidity.  
The Republic of Moldova adhered to the General Data Dissemination 
System (GDDS) in February 2003. Since then, the IMF web page 
publishes the description of the methodology used to elaborate the 
macroeconomic indicators within the relevant system and statistic 
indicators have started to be disseminated through Internet.   
The July IMF mission on observance of macroeconomic statistics 
standards and codes (ROSC mission) had as target the assessment of 
the quality of macroeconomic statistics elaborated in the Republic of 
Moldova. The mission has also assessed the compliance of 
macroeconomic indicators’ dissemination procedures with GDDS 
recommendations, as well as the compliance of statistics calculation 
practice with Data Dissemination Special Standards (DDSS).  
From 16 to 23 November 2005, IMF experts provided technical 
assistance to the Republic of Moldova within the context of country 
adherence to the system of Data Dissemination Special Standards 
(DDSS). 
Following the NBM request, the Monetary and Financial Department 
technical assistance mission of 12 to 21 December 2005 conducted a 
relevant research study and extended consultation on central bank 
independence, monetary policy and monetary operations.    
 
World Bank Group (WB) 
Based on the Decision of the Parliament no. 1107-XII of 28 July 1992, 
the Republic of Moldova adhered to the International Bank for 
Reconstruction and Development (IBRD), International Financial 
Corporation (IFC), and International Development Agency (IDA). 


Annual Report  2005                                                                                                             Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
74
The relationship with institutions within the World Bank Group over 
2005 has been based on Country Assistance Strategy (CAS) of 14 
December 2004 with regard to lending activities, economic assessment, 
and technical assistance for years 2005 to 2008. CAS has been worked 
out paying due consideration to the current economic condition of the 
Republic of Moldova, PRGF objectives and country’s aspirations to 
European integration.  
CAS provides a flexible financing framework for 2005 to 2008, which 
depends on the economic situation of the country and varies within 
US$ 90.0 million to US$ 137.0 million under IDA concession 
financing conditions.  
On 16 June 2005 the World Bank approved a US$ 8.548 million loan 
within the Public Financial Management Project and an additional loan 
of US$ 3.0 million related to the First Cadastre Project. Both loans 
have been extended under IDA standard conditions: a service charge of 
0.75% per year; with a maturity of 40 years; and a 10-year grace 
period. The Public Financial Management Project has been launched 
for purposes of improving the budgetary transparency and ensuring the 
compatibility of budgetary classification with the international system 
of governmental financial statistics. The total project cost is US$ 
15.331 million and includes, as well, the local contribution and grants 
by the Government of the Netherlands and the Swedish International 
Development Agency (SIDA). The First Cadastre Additional Financing 
for the First Cadastre Project shall finance activities related to the 
enlargement of the scope and access to real estate registration system, 
consolidation of data of all territorial cadastral offices and 
enhancement of information flow.  
With the view to supporting investments in the private sector, the 
World Bank approved on 27 October 2005 a Competitiveness 
Enhancement Project in a total value of US$ 9.8 million, of which US$ 
4.9 million is financed through a grant and US$ 4.9 million – through 
an IDA standard loan. Financial resources shall be aimed at financing 
four categories of activities, including: improvements in the business 
environment (including introducing of Regulatory Impact Assessment 
(RIA) in the legislative process of Moldova); modernisation of the 
metrology, standardization, testing and quality infrastructure (MSTQ); 
improving access of enterprises to MSTQ; and improving access to 
finance (through establishment of a credit history database, relevant 
regulatory framework, etc.).

system

private

International Development Agency (IDA) 
The stock of IDA credits as of 31 December 2005 amounted to US$ 
209.2 million; total disbursements over the year totalled US$ 23.6 
million.  
 
European Bank for Reconstruction and Development (EBRD) 
The Strategy for the Republic of Moldova for the following two years 
has been approved at the EBRD Board on 26 July 2005. 
In 2005 EBRD approved a credit of US$ 0.9 million directed to 
ProCredit Moldova, aimed at facilitating small and medium 
enterprises’ access to financing. 
In the first half of 2005 EBRD concluded the negotiations with 
“Azertrans” SRL (subsidiary company of Azerpetrol Group, Republic 
of Azerbaijan) on the restructuring of Giurgiulesti Petrol Terminal 
Project. Following transaction conclusion, the state payment guarantee 
provided as in accordance with the Performance State Guarantee 
Agreement has been annulled.  
In 2005, the state payment guarantee for the restructuring of the 
overdue indebtedness of Vininvest SRL Company related to Wine 
Export Promotion Project has been annulled, as well.  
The total stock of EBRD direct and private credits (under state 
guarantee and with no state guarantee) as of 31 December 2005 
amounted to US$ 67.2 million, of which direct debt and private credits 
for which state guarantees have been issued over previous years 
accounted for 39.1%; and private non-guaranteed debt – for 60.9%.  
Sector-wise, the EBRD credit portfolio as of 31 December 2005 
includes as follows: credits to infrastructure - 27.0%; to financial 
institutions –26.7%; to energy sector –24.4%; to transportations –
19.8%; to small business financing – 1.3%; to communications – 0.5%; 
and to agrobusiness – 0.3%.

76
Disbursements over 2005 under EBRD projects in the private sector 
(credits with no state guarantee) totalled US$ 10.8 million, and have 
been integrally directed towards the banking sector. Total payments in 
2005 amounted to US$ 32.7 million, including: private non-guaranteed 
loans - US$ 20.0 million; external commitments under state guarantee 
– US$ 11.5 million; and direct debt servicing – US$ 1.2 million.                   
 
European Union (EU) 
The legal framework of cooperation relationship between the Republic 
of Moldova and the European Union embodies the Partnership and 
Cooperating Agreement (PCA) signed on 28 November 1994 (in effect 
from 1 July 1998). 
The Actions Plan Republic of Moldova – EU, which is the basic 
document regulating the implementation of PCA provisions has been 
approved following the Decision of the Government of the Republic of 
Moldova no. 356 of 22 April 2005.  
Under the scope of PCA implementation, the National Bank of 
Moldova participated at the updating of the National Program on 
Implementation of Measures of Actions Plan RM – EU, in particular of 
measures pertinent to NBM activity related to objective 19 “Medium –
Term 
Economic 
Growth 
Consolidation 
and 
Sustainability”, 
compartment “Functional Market Economy”, etc. The National Bank 
submitted in 2005 monthly, quarterly and semester reports on the 
implementation of the Actions plan RM – EU approved on 22 February 
2005 by the RM – EU Common Council for Cooperation.  
Experts of the National Bank of Moldova have participated, as well, at 
the works of the inter-ministerial Commission for Social and Economic 
Issues and of the National Commission for European Integration on the 
implementation of the Actions Plan RM – EU.  
In September 2005 the National Bank of Moldova submitted relevant 
data on NBM activity for the meeting of 6-7 October 2005 of the RM – 
EU Parliamentary Cooperation Committee, which was held in 
Chisinau.  
The financial assistance extended by the European Union to the 
Republic of Moldova until 31 December 2005 has been channelled 
through three main instruments: financing of the deficit of the balance 
of payments via loans and grants; TACIS program; and financing of 
medicines’ and food items’ acquisition. In 2005 the Republic of 
Moldova received a grant of EURO 18.3 million within the Food 
Security Program.

European Council Development Bank  
The Parliament of the Republic of Moldova has ratified, following 
Parliamentary Decision no. 77-XVI of 12 February 2005, the 
Agreement – credit framework between the European Council 
Development Bank and the Republic of Moldova, which has been 
signed on 5 January 2005 and provides a loan of EURO 6.0 million to 
finance the project of Transfusion Security in the Republic of Moldova.   
 
Group of Banking Supervisors from the Central and Eastern Europe 
The National Bank of Moldova joined the Group of Banking 
Supervisors from the Central and Eastern Europe in June 1996 
following the signing of the Agreement on the Organization and 
Management of the BSCEE Group (with further modifications and 
completions). As in accordance with this Agreement, annual 
contributions related to operational costs of the Secretariat of BSCEE 
Group per member amount to Euro 1 thousand to be paid on an annual 
basis.  
The membership of the BSCEE Group assists the Republic of Moldova 
in duly implementing the Basel standards on banking supervision. The 
application by NBM of Basel core principle for banking supervision 
determines the promotion of an adequate and efficient banking 
supervision, which adds up to the maintenance of the banking sector 
financial stability, the enhancement of bank’ competitiveness in the 
international market, and the safeguarding of domestic depositors’ 
interests. Within the Financial Sector Assessment Program, which has 
been conducted by experts of the World Bank and the International 
Monetary Fund in 2004, special emphasis has been put on detailed 
assessment of Moldova’s compliance with Basel Committee core 
principles on efficient banking supervision.  
Under the scope of membership of the BSCEE Group, the National 
Bank participates at activities related to experience exchange among 
Group members, continuous staff training, elaboration of new 
standards, as well as receives methodological assistance in the field of 
banking prudential regulation.  
The cooperation within the BSCEE Group contributes to the 
consolidation of the domestic banks’ competitiveness, the capacity of 


Annual Report  2005                                                                                                             Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
78
thereof to attract investments, including external ones, and thus to 
enhance banks’ capasity to satisfy relevant needs for banking services 
of the economic sector of the country.  
 
International Fund for Agricultural Development (IFAD)  
The Rural Finance and Small Enterprise Development Project (IFAD I) 
in a total value of US$ 8.1 million was concluded on 31 December 
2005. The Project provided direct credit disbursements, acquisition of 
relevant equipment and extension of technical assistance to small 
agricultural enterprises within rural areas.  
Agricultural Revitalization Project  (IFAD II) in a total value of US$ 
15.0 million is the second project implemented by IFAD for purposes 
of poverty eradication and increase of living standard within rural 
areas. The project is harmonised with objectives within the Moldovan 
Village Strategy of the Republic of Moldova. 
Loans within these projects have been extended under concession 
conditions at a service charge of 0.75% per year; with a maturity term 
of 40 years; and a grace period of 10 years. Moldova’s indebtedness to 
IFAD as of 31 December 2005 amounted to US$ 8.3 million; total 
disbursements over the year valued US$ 1.4 million.  
 
Cooperation with Central Banks of Other States 
In 2005 the National Banks has actively cooperated with the central 
banks of other states.  
Based on established relationship, NBM cooperated with the Central 
Bank of the Check Republic in the field of new investment instruments 
of foreign exchange reserves management. The NBM staff participated 
at two activities within IT audit experience exchange with the Central 
Bank of Latvia and the Central Bank of Armenia. Audit and internal 
control experience has been shared, as well, with De Nederlandsche 
Bank N.V. The cooperation with the Central Bank of Spain embodied 
the audit of foreign exchange reserves management.  
Within the context of bilateral cooperation, the National Bank of 
Moldova extended technical assistance to the National Bank of 
Kyrgyzstan on the methodology used to assess economic migrants’ 
remittances.  
NBM cooperated with the Agency of the Republic of Kazakhstan for 
the Financial Market and Financial Organizations Regulation and

Supervision with the view to elaborating the Banking Supervision 
Cooperation Agreement. 
Over 2005 the National Bank of Moldova cooperated, as well, with the 
Central Banks of the Republic of Kazakhstan, of the Russian 
Federation, of Romania, the National Bank of Poland in the field of 
banking supervision.  
The NBM staff benefited over 2005 of training courses held by the 
following central banks: Deutsche Bundesbank, Banque de France, 
Swiss National Bank, Bank of England, De Nederlandsche Bank N.V., 
the National Bank of the Czech Republic, the National Bank of Poland, 
Bank of Latvia, and the Central Bank of the Russian Federation.

80
Banking Supervision and Regulation  
As of 31 December 2005 there were 16 joint stock commercial banks, 
including two branches of foreign banks, authorised by the National 
Bank of Moldova to conduct financial activity in the territory of the 
Republic of Moldova (one bank was under liquidation as in 
accordance with the relevant decision of bank’s shareholders). The 
total number of banking institutions accounted for 928 units, of which 
212 branches, 271 representative offices and 445 agencies. Over 2005 
banks opened 18 branches and 78 representative offices and closed 16 
representative offices and one branch.  
As of 31 December 2005 there were 8476 employees activating in the 
banking sector of the Republic of Moldova; the number of thereof 
rose, as compared to the previous year, by 578 persons.     
  
New Prudential Regulations  
Over 2005 the National Bank of Moldova undertook a series of 
measures aimed at consolidating the banking system of the country, 
mitigating risks related to banking operations, and improving the 
mechanism of banking supervision with the view to safeguarding 
depositors’ interests. 
Following the examination of recommendations included in the 
Financial Sector Assessment Program (FSAP), the National Bank 
worked out the draft Law on Modification and Completion of a series 
of legislative acts related to the activity of the banking system. As of 
21 October 2005 the Parliament of the Republic of Moldova approved 
Law no. 249-XVI on Modification and Completion of the Law on 
Financial Institutions no. 550-XIII of 21 July 1995 and of Article 24 
of Law no. 199-XIV of 18 November 1998 on Securities’ Market 
(Official Monitor of the Republic of Moldova no. 154-156 of 18 
November 2005). Relevant modifications shall contribute to the 
establishment of adequate transparency of banks’ owners; stimulation 
of strategic investors’ attraction from abroad; restriction of 
transactions with banks’ stocks belonging to persons placed in 
countries where disclosure of information on entrepreneurial activity 
is forbidden; avoidance of conflicts of interest and ensurance of due 
supervision and efficient and adequate control over bank’s executive 
body; non-admittance of banks’ implication in excessive risks; and 
toughening of sanctions for violation of effective legislation. 
FSAP conclusions and recommendations have been paid, as well, due 
consideration while elaborating the Strategy of the Government and 


Annual Report 2005                                                                                                            Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
81
the National Bank of Moldova on the Development of the Financial 
Sector of the Republic of Moldova for 2005 – 2010, as well as the 
National Program for the Implementation of the Actions Plan 
Republic of Moldova – EU.  
The consolidation of the banking sector has a critical importance for 
due assurance of a sustained and stable functioning of this sector. 
Under this scope, NBM established new requirements towards capital 
value. Thus, until 31 December 2005 banks were provided to 
gradually increase their Tier I Capital up to the level of 50.0, 100.0 
and 150.0 million lei, depending on the category of held license. With 
the view to conducting adequate supervision of banks’ observance of 
minimum required capital level, NBM cooperated with authorised 
banks as to perform relevant actions (including planning of thereof) 
related to banks’ compliance with new capital requirements. Based on 
financial reports submitted to the National Bank of Moldova by 
commercial banks, it has been concluded that over 2005 all the banks 
conformed to capital requirements.  
With the view to maintaining the stability of the banking system, the 
National Bank of Moldova has further upgraded the methods of 
banking supervision, paying due attention to general accepted 
standards and best practices in the field of banking supervision, 
including to the documents of Basel Committee on effective banking 
supervision. 
Thus, considering that over the last years commercial banks actively 
extended microcrediting services, NBM has specified the procedure of 
classification of risks related to provision of credits under 30.0 
thousand lei. Within such classification banks have, along with 
supervising the fulfilment of credit reimbursement terms, to assess 
internal control procedures related to credit provision, supervision and 
reimbursement.  
NBM modified and completed the Regulation on Requirements 
towards Banks’ Administrators with the view to bringing these 
provisions in line with the effective legislation, as well as to 
adequately settle disorders traced out following the generalisation of 
the supervision practice.  
The National Bank has also determined the procedure of notifying the 
National Bank of Moldova on opening by foreign banks of 
representative offices within the territory of the Republic of Moldova.  
To mitigate eventual risks of banks to a person or to a group of 
persons acting in common and to affiliated persons, NBM modified 
and completed the Regulation on Large Credits and on Conclusion of 


Annual Report 2005                                                                                                            Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
82
Agreements with Affiliated Persons. Thus, the application scope of 
prudential limits related to banks’ transactions with a person or a 
group of persons acting in common, as well as to banks’ transactions 
with affiliated persons shall be extended, emphasising banks’ 
exposures to these very persons. As a result, along with extended 
credits, bank’s exposure shall include, as well, bank’s interest in the 
capital of economic units, stocks issued by economic agents, and 
means placed with banks for maturities over 15 days.  
At the same time, to bring normative acts related to the regulation and 
supervision of activity of financial institutions in line with the 
effective legislation, the National Bank conducted modifications and 
completion of a series of regulations on: banks’ licensing; holding of 
significant interest in bank’s capital; holding by banks of interest in 
the capital of economic units; bank merging and association; opening 
of branches by banks; provision of certificates of qualification to 
banking auditors.           
The National Bank of Moldova cooperated with the National 
Commission of Securities in order to establish relevant legal 
conditions for due assurance of transparency of owners of banks’ 
shares, that are recorded and maintained by the nominal holder of 
shares. Following this cooperation the Law on Securities’ Market has 
been adequately modified.  
Cooperation relationship has been established over the reported year 
between banks’ supervision bodies with the supervision body of the 
securities’ market and the supervision body of the insurance segment. 
Thus, the insurance supervision body provides information on the 
development of the insurance market, including on the financial 
condition of insurance companies in which banks have relevant 
interest. This information allows the evaluation of these companies’ 
impact upon banks’ financial condition.  
Following established collaboration between the banking supervisory 
body with the supervisory body of securities’ market, the National 
Bank of Moldova received from the National Commission of 
Securities relevant information on banks’ activity in the securities’ 
market; this information has formed the basis for the evaluation of 
commercial banks’ activity as professional participants of the 
securities’ market. NBM cooperated, as well, with NCS for purposes 
of authorising banks to conduct professional activities in the 
securities’ market.  
Concomitantly, NBM enlarged its cooperation with the Ministry of 
Finance with the view to assuring adequate exchange of information 
with regard to the activity of microfinancing organizations. Such 


Annual Report 2005                                                                                                            Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
83
information is used to conduct the analysis of these organizations’ 
impact upon the banking system of the Republic of Moldova.  
To adequately monitor banks’ activity related to transactions of 
thereof in the international market and to determine country risk faced 
by the bank, NBM improved the supervision mechanism within this 
field. Thus, risks related to placement of banks’ money means in 
foreign banks have been assessed using the country rating set by 
Standard and Poor’s (S&P).  
At the same time, within relevant controls, NBM inspectors assessed 
bank’s internal control systems and determined whether risk 
evaluation policies and procedures (country risk and transfer risk, 
interest rate risk, exchange rate risk, etc.) are adequate and are 
effectively implemented. If drawbacks were traced out, relevant 
measures to further improve such systems have been recommended.    
  
Assets of Commercial Banks   
The development of the banking system of the Republic of Moldova 
over 2005 denoted positive dynamics with trend profile. Total assets 
of the banking sector rose by 4712.2 
million lei (35.4%) and amounted, as of 31 
December 2005, to 18007.7 million lei. 
Adequate increase has been also noted in 
the ratio of assets to GDP – from 41.6% up 
to 49.0% (chart no. 75). The augmentation 
of total assets of the banking sector was due 
to the enlargement by 4269.6 million lei 
(39.8%) of banks’ liabilities and by 442.6 
million lei (17.2%) of share capital.  
Assets of large and medium banks 
increased by 4524.4 million lei (48.3%) and 
270.7 million lei (9.6%) respectively; while 
those of small banks reduced by 82.9 
million lei (7.5%). Concomitantly, the 
weight of assets of large banks in total assets rose by 6.7 percentage 
points on the account of the decrease by 4.1 and 2.6 percentage points, 
respectively, of the weight of assets of medium and small banks (chart 
no. 72). 
Within assets’ structure rises have been registered in all asset 
components. Cash volumes enlarged by 208.7 million lei (28.4%); due 
from banks and from NBM – by 1545.0 million lei (48.2%); securities 
– by 425.3 million lei (43.9%); other assets – by 171.8 million lei

of credit portfolio.

Capital of Commercial Banks   
Tier I Capital represents the part of the total regulatory capital towards 
which the minimal value required for financial activities’ performance 
is established as in accordance with Article 26 of the Law on Financial 
Institutions. 
As of 31 December 2005, Tier I Capital reached the value of 2871.2 
million lei, broadening by 437.1 million lei (18.0%). The relevant 
dynamics of Tier I Capital illustrates the continuous trend of banking 
system consolidation. As in accordance with financial reports 
submitted by banks, as of 31 December 2005, in all commercial banks 
the values of Tier I Capital complied with the category of held license.

Total regulatory capital (TRC) includes Tier I Capital and Tier II 
Capital except equity interest in other banks that hold the license of 
the National Bank of Moldova. 
Total regulatory capital grew over 2005 by 437.1 million lei (18.0%). 
The dynamics of Tier I Capital and of the total regulatory capital over 
the reported period is shown in 
chart no. 77.

of shareholders in an amount of 
54.0 million lei. This situation 
added up to the sustained growth of 
banking system’s stability degree. 
The average risk weighted capital adequacy (ratio of total regulatory 
capital to risk-weighted assets) within the banking system reached, as 
of 31 December 2005, a high level of 27.2%. (The minimal level in 
the Republic of Moldova is 12.0%, the level set by the Basel 
Committee for banking supervision indicates a more reduced level of 
8.0%). The reduction of this indicator by 4.2 percentage points as 
compared to the beginning of the year signals a better capacity of 
commercial banks to administrate the lending activity.

Liabilities of Commercial Banks   
As of 31 December 2005 liabilities of commercial banks amounted to 
14997.9 million lei, augmenting from the end of 2004 by 4269.6 
million lei (39.8%). Such liabilities expansion was mainly due to the 
increase of the value of deposits∗ by 4232.7 million lei (44.5%), which 
certifies credibility in the banking system. 
Growing trends could be noted over 2005 in all liability articles, 
except other loans, which reduced by 101.6 million lei (10.8%). 
Deposits by individuals rose by 2036.4 million lei (41.9%); deposits 
by legal persons – by 1978.4 million lei (45.4%); money means owed 
to banks – by 217.8 million lei (77.0%); other liabilities – by 138.6 
million lei (48.3%).

Payments System   
 
As in accordance with the provisions of the Law on the National Bank 
of Moldova, the National Bank conducts the supervision of the 
payments system in the Republic of Moldova and facilitates the 
efficient functioning of the interbank payments system. 
 
Interbank Payments System  
With the view to duly fulfilling relevant legislatively vested 
attributions and tasks assigned under the Strategy of the Government 
and the National Bank of Moldova on the development of the 
financial sector of the Republic of Moldova for the years 2005 – 
2010, and for purposes of bringing the interbank payments system of 
the Republic of Moldova in line with relevant international standards 
and practices and of accelerating non-cash payments within the 
country, in 2005 the National Bank initiated the implementation of 
the new automated interbank payments system. 
Within the implementation stage of the new automated interbank 
payments system, the National Bank of Moldova has revised, as well, 
the concept of non-cash payment instruments use and of remote 
electronic servicing.    
Under this scope, NBM worked out new regulations on remote 
banking servicing systems, credit transfer, direct debiting and 
incontestable collection and specified the payment instruments and 
documents to be used upon performance of non-cash payments within 
the territory of the Republic of Moldova.  
The number of payment documents has been reduced for purposes of 
bettering the use of above-mentioned instruments. Thus, credit 
transfer shall be performed based on payment orders, direct debiting – 
based on payment request, and incontestable collection – based on 
collection order.  
NBM enlarged, as well, the application scope of remote banking 
servicing with the view to ensuring a broader range of services 
rendered to banks’ clients within these systems. 
The interbank payments system processed over 2005 10.6 million 
payment documents, totalling 120300.4 million lei. As compared to 
the previous year, the number of processed payment documents raised 
by 27.3%, and the value of thereof – by 26.7%. 
 
      


Annual Report 2005                                                                                   Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
95
 
Systems of Payments by Card  
In 2005 the National Bank of Moldova approved new regulations in 
the field of banking cards.  
These regulations have been approved with the view to improving the 
normative basis related to the activity of commercial banks and 
clients of thereof within payments systems by banking cards as in 
accordance with EU norms.  
NBM has paid special attention to the updating of requirements 
related to conclusion by commercial banks of rules on banking cards’ 
usage and of documents confirming performance of transactions by 
banking cards, rights and obligations of banks, as well as of holders of 
banking cards. The National Bank has also improved supervision and 
reporting procedures related to banks’ activity within payments 
systems by card.  
As in accordance with new regulations, cards issued by issuing banks 
have been classified, depending on use purpose, into personal cards 
and business cards. Such classification significantly stimulated the 
process of performing payments by banking cards by legal entities.   
 
The number of banking cards in circulation as of 31 December 2005 
totalled 432.5 thousand units, surpassing by 27.1% the number in the 
previous year.  
 
Over 2005 year holders of banking cards issued by authorised banks 
conducted in the territory of the Republic of Moldova 6.6 million 
transactions in a total value of 3771.1 million lei, denoting a 28.3%-
increase in volume terms and a 31.2%-increase in value terms as 
compared to 2004.  
 
Holders of banking cards issued abroad performed 351.8 thousand 
transactions within the territory of the Republic of Moldova, totalling 
653.5 million lei, which is by 8.6% and by 31.3%, respectively, more 
than the number and value registered in 2004.

NBM Personnel and Professional Training  
 
As of 31 December 2005 the staff of the National Bank of Moldova 
was completed at the level of 96.7% (445 employees out of 460). 
There are 9 persons with doctorates in economics and technical 
sciences employed at the National Bank of Moldova.   
 
In 2005, 36 employees have been promoted; around 64.0% of them 
are under 30 years old. Young employees constituted the NBM staff 
base: 235 employees are under 40 years old, accounting for 52.8% of 
staff.  
 


Annual Report 2005                                                                                   Activity of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
97
Over the reported year 83 employees of the National Bank 
participated at training courses. The participation of over 33.0% of 
NBM personnel at various training events contributed to the 
improvement and the modernisation of the activity of the National 
Bank of Moldova.  
 
 
Internal Audit  
 
The activity of internal audit is organized and performed as in 
accordance with the Regulation on the Activity of Internal Audit, in 
compliance with the International Standards on Internal Audit 
Professional Practice issued by Internal Auditors Institute, as well as 
the standards issued by Information Systems Audit and Control 
Association (ISACA).  
Over the reported year the Internal Audit Department conducted the 
following organizational activities: i) development of methodological 
basis related to NBM internal audit; ii) implementation of internal 
audit management software - AutoAudit; iii) pilot implementation of 
integrated audit (financial audit, operational audit and IT audit); iv) 
elaboration and application of a risk procedure aimed at annual 
planning of audit activities; v) improvement of the monitoring process 
of findings and recommendations provided in relevant reports of 
previous audits; and vi) initiation of recording of incidents, 
irregularities and errors in the NBM subdivisions’ activity processes.  
 
The Internal Audit Department conducted over 2005 audit activities, 
special investigations, and evaluations of NBM financial statements.    


Annual Report  2005                                                                                      Fianancial Statement of the National Bank of Moldova 
 
98
3. Financial Statement of the National Bank of Moldova   
 
COMMENT 
 
On the Financial Statements of the National Bank of Moldova  
As of 31 December 2005  
 
The Internal Audit Department has, based on the Law on the National Bank of 
Moldova, Art. 33(4) d) and the Regulation on Activity of Internal Audit 
Department approved following the Decision of the Council of 
Administration of the National Bank of Moldova no. 260 of 13 October 2004, 
verified the financial statements of the National Bank of Moldova as of 31 
December 2005, which include: balance sheet; income statement; cash flow 
statement; Bank’s capital; as well as explanatory notes.  
 
Following the above-mentioned verification, the Internal Audit Department 
states as follows:  
 
• The balance sheet amounted, as of 31 December 2005, to 12 789 807 
thousand lei. 
• The Bank’s capital totalled 200 000 thousand lei.  
• Special Fund has, based on provisions of Art. 20 (1) of the Law on the 
National Bank of Moldova, been increased in 2005 from 277 024 thousand lei 
up to 319 175 thousand lei following the 20%-transfer from Bank’s income.  
• Foreign exchange unrealised losses related to the reported year amounted to 
198 163 thousand lei, of which 72 200 thousand lei have been covered with 
Special Reserve means from unrealised incomes from foreign exchange 
transactions.   
• Incomes and expenses related to banking operations and resulted from 
operational activity as in accordance with the calculation method amounted, 
respectively, to 383 328 thousand lei and 173 224 thousand lei. 
•  The net profit available for distribution amounted to 210 751 thousand lei, 
which has been adjusted with the realised surplus from revaluation of fixed 
assets – 647 thousand lei.  
 
Based on above-stated, the Internal Audit Department confirms the 
authenticity of the NBM bookkeeping data in all significant aspects: balance 
sheet; income statement; cash flow statement; Bank’s capital; and reserve 
fund as of 31 December 2005.                                                                                      
                                                    Deputy Controller General  


 
 
99
 
 
 
 
AUDITORS’ REPORT  
 
 
To the National Bank of Moldova 
 
 
We have audited the accompanying financial statements of the National Bank of Moldova (“the Bank”), which 
comprise the balance sheet as of 31 December 2005 and the income statement, statement of changes in equity 
and cash flow statement for the year then ended. These financial statements are the responsibility of the Bank’s 
management. Our responsibility is to express an opinion on these financial statements based on our audit. 
 
We conducted our audit in accordance with International Standards on Auditing. Those standards require that we 
plan and perform the audit to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the financial statements are free of 
material misstatement. An audit includes examining, on a test basis, evidence supporting the amounts and 
disclosures in the financial statements. An audit also includes assessing the accounting principles used and 
significant estimates made by management, as well as evaluating the overall financial statement presentation. 
We believe that our audit provides a reasonable basis for our opinion. 
 
In our opinion the financial statements referred to above give a true and fair view of the financial position of the 
Bank as of 31 December 2005 and of its financial performance and its cash flows for the year then ended in 
accordance with International Financial Reporting Standards.

NATIONAL BANK OF MOLDOVA 
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS 
for the year ended 31 December 2005 
(all amounts are expressed in thousand Moldovan Lei “MDL ‘000”) 
 
 104
1. Corporate information 
The National Bank of Moldova (the Bank or NBM) was established in 1991. The activities of the Bank are as 
follows: 
• 
to formulate and to promote the state monetary and foreign exchange policy; 
• 
to act as banker and fiscal agent of the Republic; 
• 
to conduct economic and monetary analysis and submit proposals to the Government on the basis of such 
analysis, and publish the results of such analysis; 
• 
to license, supervise and regulate the activity of financial institutions; 
• 
to provide credit facilities to banks and state. 
• 
to supervise the system of payments of the Republic and to facilitate efficient functioning of inter-bank 
system of payments. 
• 
to act as the sole issuer of domestic currency in the Republic; 
• 
to hold and manage foreign exchange reserves of the Republic; 
• 
to undertake, in the name of the Republic, responsibilities and perform transactions resulting from the 
participation of the Republic of Moldova in the activity of international public institutions in the banking, 
credit and monetary spheres pursuant to conditions of international agreements; and 
• 
to settle the balance of payments of the Republic. 
The activity of the Bank is regulated by the Law on the National Bank of Moldova approved by the Parliament in 
1995. In accordance with the Law on the National Bank of Moldova the Bank is an autonomous organization that 
reports to the Parliament of the Republic of Moldova. The Bank in cooperation with the Government of the 
Republic of Moldova formulates and implements the monetary and foreign exchange policy.  
As at 31 December 2005 the number of positions available at the Bank, including vacancies, was 460 (31 December 
2004: 468), out of which the number of employees was 445 persons (459 persons as of 31 December 2004). 
The registered office of the Bank is located at Renasterii Avenue 7, Chisinau, Republic of Moldova. 
2. Basis of preparation 
The financial statements are presented in Moldovan lei (“MDL”), the currency of the country in which the Bank 
operates.  The Bank maintains its books and records in accordance with the International Financial Reporting 
Standards and the Law on the National Bank and prepares its statements in accordance with them. The financial 
statements are prepared under the historic cost convention as modified by the revaluation of property, plant and 
equipment, the fair valuation of the financial assets at fair value through profit and loss, and finnacial assets 
available for sale.  
3. Significant accounting policies 
a. Basis of accounting 
These financial statements are prepared in conformity with International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS), 
which comprise standards and interpretations approved by the International Accounting Standards Board (IASB), 
and International Accounting Standards and Standing Interpretation Committee interpretations approved by the 
International Accounting Standards Committee (IASC) that remain in effect. 
The Bank has not applied IFRS 7 “Financial Instruments-Disclosures” that has been issued but is not yet effective. 
This Standard supersedes IAS 30 and disclosure requirements of IAS 32 and is effective for annual periods 
beginning on or after January 1, 2007. 
 


NATIONAL BANK OF MOLDOVA 
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS 
for the year ended 31 December 2005 
(all amounts are expressed in thousand Moldovan Lei “MDL ‘000”) 
 
 105
3. 
Significant accounting policies (continued) 
b. Revaluation of foreign exchange stocks and transactions 
Foreign currency transactions are recorded at the exchange rate on the date of the transaction.  At the balance sheet 
date, monetary assets and liabilities denominated in foreign currencies are reported using the closing exchange rate. 
The year end and average rates for the period were:

Exchange rate differences arising on the settlement of the transactions at rates different from the weighted average 
foreign exchange rate of stocks in foreign currency, and unrealized foreign exchange differences on unsettled 
foreign currency monetary assets and liabilities, are recognized in the income statement. 
c. Comparative figures 
The financial statements presentation for some items has changed in 2005. Accordingly, the comparatives have been 
amended to reflect the presentational changes. The following notes were amended- notes 7, 10 with the amount of 
MDL 000’380, notes 21,22 - MDL’000 2,269, notes 23, 24 - MDL’000 477. 
d. Use of estimates 
The preparation of financial statements in accordance with International Financial Reporting Standards requires 
management to make estimates and assumptions that affect the amounts and balances reported in the financial 
statements and accompanying notes. These estimates are based on information available as of the date of the 
financial statements. Actual results, therefore, could differ from those estimates. 
e. Cash and cash equivalents 
For the purpose of the cash flow statement, cash and cash equivalents comprise cash in hand, current accounts and 
short-term placements at other banks, treasury bills and other short – term highly liquid investments, with less than 3 
months maturity from the date of acquisition. 
f. Due from banks 
These are stated at amortized cost, less any provisions for impairment. 
g. Due from International Financial Institutions 
The amounts recorded in the balance sheet as “Due from International Financial Institutions” represents mainly the 
quota of the Republic of Moldova in the International Monetary Fund (“IMF”). This amount is established in 
Special Drawing Rights (“SDR”) but it is administered in MDL. 
h. Financial assets 
The Bank classifies its financial assets into the following categories: financial assets at fair value through profit and 
loss account, loans originated by the Bank; held-to-maturity investments; and available-for-sale financial assets. 


NATIONAL BANK OF MOLDOVA 
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS 
for the year ended 31 December 2005 
(all amounts are expressed in thousand Moldovan Lei “MDL ‘000”) 
 
 106
3. 
Significant accounting policies (continued) 
h. Financial assets (continued) 
Financial assets at fair value through profit and loss account 
Financial assets at fair value through profit and loss account are securities, which were either acquired for generating 
a profit from short term fluctuations in price, or are securities included in a portfolio in which a pattern of short term 
profit taking exists. After initial recognition at fair value, trading securities are remeasured at fair value based on 
quoted bid prices. All related realised and unrealised gains or losses are recognised in trading income. Interest 
income on trading securities is included in interest income. All purchases and sales of treasury securities that require 
delivery within the time frame established by regulation or market convention are recognised at settlement date.  
The Bank includes in this category securities issued by non-residents with coupon or discount and purchased from 
the foreign markets.  
Available for sale financial assets 
All the financial assets, which are not classified at fair value through profit and loss account, as held to maturity or 
as originated by the Bank, are included in available for sale securities. All purchases and sales of securities that 
require delivery within the time frame established by regulation or market convention are recognised at settlement 
date. Available for sale assets are recognised initially at fair value (including transaction costs). Subsequent to initial 
recognition, they are remeasured at their fair value unless fair value cannot be determined in which case they are 
carried at amortised cost. 
Fair values are based on quoted bid prices or amounts derived from cash flow models and ratios which reflect the 
specific circumstances of the issuer.  
A gain or loss on an available-for-sale financial asset are recognized directly in equity through the statement of 
changes in equity, except for impairment losses until the financial asset is derecognized, at which time the 
cumulative gain or loss previously recognized in equity is recognized in profit or loss. 
Interest calculated using the effective interest method is recognized in profit or loss. 
The Bank includes in this category treasury bills issued by the Ministry of Finance. 
Loans originated by the Bank 
Loans originated by the Bank represent loans where money is provided directly to the borrower and are recognized 
when the cash is advanced to borrowers. They are initially recorded at cost, which is the fair value of the cash 
disbursed, and are subsequently measured at amortized cost using the effective interest rate method.   
Held to maturity investments (HTM) 
HTM investments are non-derivative financial assets with fixed or determinable payments and fixed maturities that 
the Bank’s management has the positive intention and ability to hold to maturity. Were the Bank to sell other than a 
significant amount of HTM assets, the entire category would be tainted and reclassified as available for sale. 
The Bank does not currently classify any items in this category. 


NATIONAL BANK OF MOLDOVA 
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS 
for the year ended 31 December 2005 
(all amounts are expressed in thousand Moldovan Lei “MDL ‘000”) 
 
 107
3. 
Significant accounting policies (continued) 
i. Provisions for impairment of the loans  
If there is objective evidence that the Bank will not be able to collect all amounts due (principal and interest) such 
loans are considered impaired. 
The amount of the impairment loss is the difference between the carrying value and estimated recoverable value, 
calculated as updated value of cash flows estimated for recovery including the amounts recoverable from collaterals, 
updated based on initial interest of the instrument. 
The provision for impairement of loans covers also the situation when objective evidences that probable losses shall  
occur wth regard to credit portfolio elements as of the balance sheet date. 
The carrying amount of the asset is reduced to its estimated recoverable amount by a charge to income statement 
through the use of a provision for loan impairment  A write off is made when all or part of a loan is deemed 
uncollectable. Write offs are charged against previously established provisions and reduce the principal amount of a 
loan. Recoveries of loans written off in earlier periods are included in income. 
If the amount of the impairment subsequently decreases due to an event occurring after the write-down, the release 
of the provision is credited to the provision for loan losses expense. 
j. Sale and repurchase agreements 
Securities sold with a simultaneous commitment to repurchase at a specified future date (Repos) continue to be 
recognised in the balance sheet as securities and are measured in accordance with respective accounting policies.  
The liability for amounts received under these agreements is included in due to Moldovan Government.  The 
difference between sale and repurchase price is treated as interest expense using the effective yield method.   
k. Property and equipment  
Property and equipment are stated at cost or revalued amount less accumulated depreciation and any impairment 
loss.  
In 1996 the Company has restated the items of property, plant and equipment that were acquired prior to 1 January 
1996 by applying the set of indices elaborated by the Moldovan Government. The indices provided by the Ministry 
of Finance of the Republic of Moldova are applied to the net book value of assets in order to reflect changes in 
prices. The indices varied according to asset type and acquisition date.  No residual value has been considered. 
Expenses for repairs and maintenance are charged to operating expenses as incurred.  Subsequent expenditure on 
property and equipment is only recognised as an asset when the expenditures improves the condition of the asset 
beyond the originally assessed standard of performance. 
Where the carrying amount of an asset is greater than the estimated recoverable amount, it is written down to its 
recoverable amount.  Gains and losses on disposals of property and equipment are determined by reference to their 
carrying amount and are taken to income. 
Depreciation is computed on a straight-line basis using the following rates specified for each depreciable asset to 
decrease the cost of each asset to their residual values over their estimated useful life: 
 
 


NATIONAL BANK OF MOLDOVA

for the year ended 31 December 2005

l. Intangibles 
Intangibles represent costs incurred for acquisition of computer software, amortised using the straight-line method 
over their estimated useful lives ranging from 1 to 5years. 
Where the carrying amount of an intangible asset is greater than the estimated recoverable amount, it is written 
down to its recoverable amount. 
m. National currency issued into circulation 
The national currency issued into circulation is carried at nominal value. For presentation purposes the cash on hand  
available in the Cash in Circulation Departmentof the Bank is offset against national currency issued in circulation. 
The cost of production of banknotes and coins is recorded in the income statement when the expense is in incurred. 
n. Due to banks 
Due to banks include among other the mandatory reserves required to satisfy the obligatory reserve requirements of 
the NBM, in compliance with its prudential requirement, curent accounts and deposits placed by the Moldovan 
banks in the National Bank of Moldova. 
The mandatory reserves are determined by applying the set percentages to the average daily balances of deposit 
accounts and other similar liabilities of the commercial banks, specified for that reason by the Regulation on 
Mandatory Reserves Regime. 
o. Due to International Financial Institutions 
Due to International Financial Institutions are initially recognized at fair value, being equal to their issue proceeds.  
Subsequently they are stated at amortized cost and any difference between net proceeds and the redemption value is 
recognized in the income statement over the period to maturity using the effective yield method. 
p. Capital and reserves 
Both Statutory Fund and reserves are disclosed in the balance sheet at nominal value. 
q. Income tax 
In compliance with the art 73 of the Law on the National Bank of Moldova no.548 - XIII dated 21 July 1995 the 
National Bank of Moldova is not subject to any income or profit taxes. 


NATIONAL BANK OF MOLDOVA 
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS 
for the year ended 31 December 2005 
(all amounts are expressed in thousand Moldovan Lei “MDL ‘000”) 
 
 109
 
3. 
Significant accounting policies (continued) 
r. Interest income and expenses 
Interest income and expense are recognized in the income statement for all instruments measured at cost using the 
effective interest method. 
The effective interest method is a method for computing the amortized cost of a financial asset or a financial liability 
and to allocate the interest income or expense over the relevant period. The effective interest rate is the rate that 
exactly discounts estimated future cash payments or receipts through the expected life of the financial instrument or, 
when appropriate, a shorter period to the net carrying amount of the financial asset or financial liability. When 
calculating the effective interest rate, the Bank estimates cash flows considering all contractual terms of the financial 
instrument (for example, prepayment options). The calculation includes all fees and commissions paid or received 
between the parties that are an integral part of the effective interest rate, transaction costs and all other premiums or 
discounts.  
s. Revaluation of foreign currency assets and liabilities 
Unrealized gains or losses from foreign exchange currency position represent the daily revaluations of the foreign 
currency denominated assets and liabilities held by the NBM. By virtue of its activities as a central bank for 
currency market intervention purposes, NBM maintains open currency positions at the reporting dates. 
In line with best international practices, it is considered that distribution of unrealized gains or losses from foreign 
exchange currency at the end of the year is not prudent for national banks. 
In accordance with the art.54 of the Law on the National Bank of Moldova no.548 - XIII dated 21 July 1995, the 
unrealized net gains or losses from foreign exchange currency resulting from revaluation of assets and liabilities in 
foreign currency against MDL are recorded with a corresponding amount in the special reserve. The amount 
recorded in the special reserve corresponding to the net foreign exchange losses is guaranteed by negotiable 
receivables at a market interest rate, issued by the Government of the Republic of Moldova, and held by the Bank in 
a special account; within four months after each financial year, the aggregate total amount of such receivables held 
by the National Bank is to be increased or decreased in order to restore the equivalency between that amount and the 
amount recorded in the special reserve as net foreign exchange losses as at the end of that financial year. 
t. Fiduciary activities 
The National Bank of Moldova acts as fiscal agent of the State. Assets and income arising from these activities are 
excluded from these financial statements where the National Bank of Moldova acts in the fiduciary capacity such as 
nominee or agent. 
 


NATIONAL BANK OF MOLDOVA 
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS 
for the year ended 31 December 2005 
(all amounts are expressed in thousand Moldovan Lei “MDL ‘000”) 
 
 110
3. 
Significant accounting policies (continued) 
u. Contingencies 
Contingent liabilities are not recognised in the financial statements but they are disclosed, unless the possibility of 
an outflow of resources embodying economic benefits is remote. 
A contingent asset is not recognized in the financial statements but disclosed when an inflow of economic benefits is 
probable. 
v. Provisions 
The Bank recognizes provisions when it has a present legal or constructive obligation to transfer economic benefits 
as a result of past events and a reasonable estimate of the obligation can be made. 
w. Pension costs and employees’ benefits 
The Bank makes contributions to the funds set up by the State of Moldova for pensions and health care calculated on 
the basis of salaries of all employees of the Bank. The Bank does not operate any other retirement schemes and has 
no obligation to provide further benefits to current or former employees. 
x. Offsetting financial assets and liabilities 
Financial assets and liabilities are offset and the net amount reported in the balance sheet when there is a legally 
enforceable right to set off the recognized amounts and there is an intention to settle or realize on a net basis.  
y. Events subsequent to the balance sheet date  
Post-year-end events that provide additional information about the Bank’s position at the balance sheet date 
(adjusting events) or those that indicate that the going concern assumption is not appropriate are reflected in the 
financial statements. Post-year-end events that are not adjusting events are disclosed in the notes when material. 
z. Fair value of financial instruments 
Fair value is the amount at which a financial instrument could be exchanged in a current transaction between willing 
parties, other than in a forced sale or liquidation, and is best evidenced by a quoted market price. 
The National Bank of Moldova’s short term assets are carried in the financial statements at cost which approximates  
to their fair value as these instruments have short maturity terms, they are convertible into cash and are settled 
without significant transaction costs. The National Bank of Moldova’s loans and advances are reported at amortized 
cost less an estimate for impairment. The securities of non-residents are classified as “fair value financial assets 
through the income statement” the gain or loss from their revaluation to market value is reported in the income 
statement. The State Treasury bills are classified as “available for sale” and are reported at fair value. 
The gains and losses arising from a change in the fair value of the State Treasury bills is recognised directly in 
equity, through the statement of changes in equity, except for impairment losses, until the State Tresury bills are 
derecognised at which time the cumulative gain or losses previously recognised in equity is recognised in profit and 
loss. 
The estimated fair values of financial instruments have been determined by the National Bank of Moldova using 
available market information and appropriate valuation methodologies, such as discounted cash flows techniques.  


NATIONAL BANK OF MOLDOVA 
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS 
for the year ended 31 December 2005 
(all amounts are expressed in thousand Moldovan Lei “MDL ‘000”) 
 
 111
3. 
Significant accounting policies (continued) 
z. Fair value of financial instruments (continued) 
Where discounted cash flows techniques are used, estimated future cash flows are based on management’s best 
estimates and the discount rate is a market related rate at the balance sheet date with similar terms and conditions. 
However, judgment is required to interpret market data to determine the estimated fair value. Accordingly, the 
estimates are not necessarily indicative of the amounts the Bank could realize in a current market exchange. While 
Management tried to use available market information in estimating the fair value of financial instruments issued by 
the Government of the Republic of Moldova, the market information may not be fully reflective of the value that 
could be realized under the current circumstances. 
The carrying value of financial assets and financial liabilities carried at amortized cost approximates its fair value as 
of 31 December 2005 and 2004 since the iterest rates did not change significantly over the past years. 
4. Financial risk management 
Through its normal operations, the National Bank of Moldova is exposed to a number of risks and the principal risks 
are currency, operating, credit, interest rate, and liquidity risks. The structure of the assets and liabilities is primarily 
determined by the nature of the National Bank of Moldova’s statutory functions, rather than commercial 
considerations. At the same time NBM continually manages its exposure to risk, through a variety of risk 
management techniques. Risk management of the National Bank of Moldova is regulated by its internal instructions, 
and is closely monitored by the Administrative Council of the Bank, that analyzes issues related to the monetary, 
investment and foreign exchange policy of the National Bank of Moldova, and sets limits for volumes of 
transactions. 
Operating risk is the risk of loss arising from the breakdown of internal controls. The National Bank of Moldova 
manages this risk through its Internal Audit Department, which tests and expresses the opinion on the effective 
functioning of the internal controls system of the Bank. Segregation of duties among different departments of the 
National Bank of Moldova (front-office, middle-office and back-office) is also considered as one of the mechanisms 
of managing operating risk. 
4.1 
Credit risk 
The National Bank of Moldova is subject to credit risk that is the risk that a counterparty will be unable to pay 
amounts in full when due. The Bank’s maximum exposure to credit risk, excluding the value of collateral, is 
generally reflected in the carrying value of financial assets. 
The credit risk relating to operational credit in national currency is monitored and controlled. 
In order to control the exposure to credit risk where there are repo operations as part of open market operations of 
the Bank, there are the following control elements: 
• 
settling the transaction insurance percentage (haircut) – when securities are sold at a higher interest rate 
than established on the market at that specific moment, the National Bank protects against the eventual non 
repayment by the counterpart bank; 


NATIONAL BANK OF MOLDOVA 
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS 
for the year ended 31 December 2005 
(all amounts are expressed in thousand Moldovan Lei “MDL ‘000”) 
 
 112
4. 
Financial risk management (continued) 
4.1 
Credit risk (continued) 
 
• 
requesting an additional pledge for maintaining the initial transaction insurance percentage, settled by the 
Bank, when the market price of the State securities purchased by the Bank as part of repo purchase 
transaction, has decreased significantly. 
In order to decrease the credit risk exposure related to credits granted to comercial banks, the Bank monitors the 
quality of credit portfolio and debtors financial situation, and, periodically, evaluates the impairment provisions, and 
adjusts them to reflect best current estimates. In addition the balance of current accounts placed by commercial 
banks in the Bank serves as a collateral for those loans. 
In order to decrease the credit risk exposure related to credits granted to the Bank’s personnel (current and former), 
the Bank accepts as a collateral either the properties acquired with the loans or the salaries in case the loan was 
granted for personal needs. The collateral value represents 130% of the loan, covering principal and future interests. 
4.2 
Market risk 
The market risk occurs when the value of financial instrument floats resulting from changes of market price, even 
when these changes are caused by factors specific to individual securities or factors that affect all securities on the 
market. 
The State securities are evaluated quarterly at their fair value determined based on recent auction interest rate on  the 
primary market and all the relevant amendments to market environment that may affect the related gains. 
4.3 
Interest rate risk 
The National Bank of Moldova is exposed to interest rate price risk, principally as a result of interest rate fluctuation 
on market. 
The fluctuation of interest rates on external market may affect the investment portfolio value in foreign currency as 
well as future cash flows. On external markets, the most vulnerable instruments to fluctuation of interest rates are 
the securities in foreign currency as the fluctuation of interest rates directly affects their price. 
Consequently, due to fluctuations of interest rates on external markets the negative divergences may occur between 
the interest rates of investment portfolio in foreign currency and interest rates of the Bank’s obligations in foreign 
currency. 
While managing the interest rates risk influenced by changes on the external markets, special attention is paid to the 
principal of diversification of investment portfolio by maturity and currency. 
The Bank is exposed to interest rate risk in case of the long term borrowings received from the IMF with the floating 
interest rate. 
To the extent the interest bearing assets and liabilities come due or the related interest rate is changing in different 
periods or in different amounts, the Bank pursues the interdependence of the current market interest rates. 
Meanwhile it should be mentioned that a positive yield is not always possible to achieve due to the fact that the 
levels of the assets and liabilities mentioned above are subject to the objectives of the monetary policy.

4. 
Financial risk management (continued) 
 
4.4 
Liquidity risk 
Liquidity risk is defined as the risk of loss arising due to the mismatch of the maturities of assets and liabilities. The 
maturities of assets and liabilities and the ability to replace, at an acceptable cost, interest-bearing liabilities as they 
mature, are important factors in assessing the liquidity of the National Bank of Moldova. 
Securities issued by Government of the Republic of Moldova and held by the National Bank of Moldova have a 
contractual maturity not longer than 98 days. However, at maturity, those securities are rolled over, being 
repurchased by the Government at current market rates and new securities are issued and purchased by the National 
Bank of Moldova. 
Loans granted by the National Bank of Moldova to Government also have a contractual maturity not longer than 91 
days. At maturity, interest accrued is paid to the National Bank of Moldova. They are rolled over and accordingly 
have “de facto” longer term duration.

Loans granted by International Monetary Fund

3,965,432 
The Republic of Moldova joined the IMF on August 12, 1992. The National Bank of Moldova acts as the fiscal 
agent for the conduct of the financial transactions with the IMF and as a depository for maintaining of the IMF’s 
accounts. Membership in the IMF is quota based. A member’s quota is determined upon its admission to the 
membership and is increased periodically under General Quota Reviews. The quota forms the basis for the 
member’s financial and organizational relationship with the IMF and determines, among other things, a member’s 
relative voting power, the maximum access to the IMF financing and the share of the member in any allocation of 
SDRs. 
The IMF Quota Account reflects initial and subsequent quota payments and is an asset of the member. Up to 25% is 
payable by each member to the IMF in reserve assets specified by the IMF and the remainder is due in the member’s 
own currency. 


NATIONAL BANK OF MOLDOVA 
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS 
for the year ended 31 December 2005 
(all amounts are expressed in thousand Moldovan Lei “MDL ‘000”) 
 
 117
6. 
Due from/to International Financial Institutions (continued) 
The local currency portion of the quota payment is deposited in the IMF No1 Account, the IMF No 2 Account and 
becomes the property of the IMF. The IMF No 1 account is used for the IMF’s operational transactions (purchases, 
repurchases), whereas the IMF No 2 account is used for the payment of expenses incurred by the Fund in the 
member’s currency. 
 
The Quota of the Republic of Moldova can be used by the National Bank of Moldova only upon the official 
permission of the Government of the Republic of Moldova. 
The amounts included in the NBM balance sheet as Due to International Financial Institutions mainly include the 
loans received by the NBM from the IMF. The loans are denominated in Special Drawing Rights (“SDR”), but 
disclosed in the balance sheet at the Moldovan Lei equivalent at the year-end. 
As at 31 December 2005 the Bank’s outstanding balance of loans due to the IMF is as follows: 
• 
PRGF commitment – SDR’000 27,720 
• 
EFF commitment – SDR’000 39,062 
Poverty Reduction and Growth Facility (PRGF) represents the loan granted to the poor countries with the GDP of 
less then USD 895 per person. The IMF has granted the loan of SDR’000 27,720 for a period of 10 years with a 
grace period of 5.5 years. The loan bears an interest rate of 0.5% per annum. 
Extended Fund Facility (EFF) represents the loan granted for economic growth support and is granted to economies 
suffering balance of payments deficits. The IMF has granted the loan of SDR’000 87,500 for a period of 10 years 
with a grace period of 4.5 years and a floating interest rate set on a weekly basis by the IMF. 
The loans received by NBM from the IMF from the General Resources Account are secured by a bill of exchange 
issued by the National Bank of Moldova.  
As at 31 December 2005 for the balances with the International Financial Institutions, the assets reduced by 5.25%, 
and liabilities by 11.99% compared to 31 December 2004. This is the result of appreciation of the national currency 
rate against the SDR, as well as partial repayment of the loans granted by the International Monetary Fund.

(all amounts are expressed in thousand Moldovan Lei “MDL ‘000”) 
 
 118
7. Due from Government (continued) 
The interest rate of the loans as at 31 December 2005 varied from 3.56% to 5.20% per year, comparing to 6.56% – 
9.04% per year as at 31 December 2004. 
 
The loans granted to the Government are guaranteed by state securities at the market price issued by the 
Government of the Republic of Moldova with the same due date that can be traded on the market. 
The matured loans are extended by at most 91 days, bearing the interest rate currently available on the primary 
market for the securities with the same due date. 
As at 31 December 2005, the balance of state securities held in the Bank portfolio comprises state securities issued 
and remitted to the Bank as a result of prior conversion into state securities of internal state debts purchased from the 
Bank during the previous years. 
These securities are classified as available for sale and are recorded at their fair value. . In 2004 financial statements 
the securities issued by Government were classified as investment securities available for sale. In 2005 financial 
statements the management of the Bank decided to disclose them together with other amounts due from 
Government. 
During the year 2005, the balance of state securities increased by 5.9%, due to the fact that no securities from the 
Bank’s portfolio were sold within this period. 
The average weighted interest rate for the state securities from the Bank’s portfolio re-issued during 2005 is 3.03% 
per annum (in 2004: 10.43% per annum), maximum interest rate – 6.11% per annum (in 2004: 15.71% per annum), 
minimum interest rate – 1.09% per annum (in 2004: 6.56% per annum). 
As at 31 December 2005 the average interest rate for state securities portfolio was 4.53% per annum and the market 
interest rate for similar state securities was 3.22% per annum. 
 
8. Securities due from the Moldovan Government to be issued 
Securities due from the Moldovan Government to be issued represent the value of the securities to be provided by 
the Government of the Republic of Moldova according to art. 54 of the Law on the National Bank of Moldova no. 
548 – XIII dated 21 July 1995 in order to cover the net unrealized losses on foreign exchange differences related to 
the revaluation of assets and liabilities in foreign currency compared to MDL recorded by the Bank during the year. 
In determining the amount of securities to be issued the net unrealised losses on foreign exchange differences 
recorded in the current year have been reduced by the recorded net unrealised gain on foreign exchange differences, 
which in the prior year have been transfered to a special reserve as required by art. 54 of the Law on the National 
Bank of Moldova no.548 – XIII dated 21 July 1995.

Securities issued by non-residents 
2,610,643

1,383,044 
The securities issued by non-residents held by the National Bank of Moldova represent mainly securities issued by 
entities from the United States of America and European Union. As at 31 December 2005 all securities issued by 
non-residents held in the Bank’s portfolio have the highest rating - „AAA”. 
Those securities are low risk assets and are classified as financial assets at fair value through profit and loss account. 
In 2004 financial statements those securities were classified as available for sale. In 2005 those securities were 
classified in financial assets at fair value through profit and loss account. 
During 2005, the securities issued by non-residents increased by 88.76%, as a result of increase of foreign exchange 
reserves and, respectively, increase of securities portfolio in foreign currency.  
During the reported period, the total amount of securities sold or matured denominated in foreign currency 
represented the total nominal value of USD 50 million, EUR 44.5 million and GBP 4 million respectively. 
Consequently, there were purchased securities denominated in foreign currency in the total nominal value of USD 
105 million, EUR 66 million and GBP 15 million respectively.

In accordance with art. 20 of the Law nr.548 – XIII dated 21 July 1995 on the National Bank of Moldova, the Bank 
has to distribute 20% of the profit available for distribution to the reserve fund on an annual basis, up to two times 
the value of the statutory capital. As at 31 December 2005 the amount of MDL’000 42,151 (2004: MDL ‘000 
45,952) was transferred to the reserve fund. 
Other reserves include reserves from the revaluation of tangible fixed assets of MDL’000 2,496 (31 December 2004: 
MDL’000 3,143) and available for sale securities revaluation surplus of MDL’000 442 (31 December 2004: 
MDL’000 0). 
Special reserve created in 2004 represents net unrealised gains from foreign exchange differences that in accordance 
with art. 54 c) of the Law nr.548 – XIII dated 21 July 1995 on the National Bank of Moldova should not be included 
in the retained earnings for the year available for distribution to the State budget. The special reserve has been 
utilised in 2005 (see note 8).

25. Related parties  
During the year ended at 31 December 2005, the remuneration paid to the Bank management, including middle level 
management, was MDL’000 2,961 (2004: MDL’000 2,612). 
The Bank has granted loans to its employees. The remaining balance of the loans granted to the management of the 
Bank, including middle level management, amounted to MDL’000 676 as at 31 December 2005 (31 December 
2004: MDL’000 801). 
26. Commitments and contingencies 
Litigations 
As at 31 December 2005 the Bank was involved a litigation against a former employee for the unreimbursed loan 
and litigation against the Bank. As at 31 December 2005 the Bank has recorded a provision for litigation of 
MDL’000 46, considering that the value of the provision is adequate to cover the future expenses related to the 
litigation against the Bank. For the litigation against the former employee the Bank has recorded a provision for loan 
losses as at 31 December 2005, covering 60% of the outstanding debt. 
 
Credit related commitments 
As at 31 December 2005, there were no outstanding credit related commitments.
